Figure 2.
Definition of
APCP
’s process language.

We write
a,b,c,\ldots,x,y,z,\ldots
to denote (channel)
names
(also known as
names
); by convention we use the early letters of the alphabet for the objects of output-like prefixes.
Also, we write
\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\tilde{z},\ldots
to denote sequences of names.
In
APCP
, communication is

asynchronous
(cf.
[
HT91
,
HT92
,
Bou92
]
) and

dyadic
: each communication involves the transmission of a pair of names, a message name and a continuation name.
With a slight abuse of notation, we sometimes write
x_{i}\in\tilde{x}
to refer to a specific element in the sequence
\tilde{x}
.
Also, we write
i,j,k,\ldots
to denote
labels
for choices and
I,J,K,\ldots
to denote sets of labels.
We write
X,Y,\ldots
to denote
recursion variables
, and
P,Q,\ldots
to denote processes.

{defi}

[
APCP
Syntax]

The syntax of
APCP
processes is as follows:

\displaystyle P,Q

\displaystyle\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}lr@{\kern 2em}l@{\kern 1em}lr@{}}{}::=&x[a,b]&\text{send}\kern 20.00003pt&\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}\hfil\kern 10.00002pt&x(y,z);P&\text{receive}\\
~~\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}&x[b]\triangleleft\ell&\text{selection}\kern 20.00003pt&\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}\hfil\kern 10.00002pt&x(z)\triangleright\{i:P\}_{i\in I}&\text{branch}\\
~~\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}&(\bm{\nu}xy)P&\text{restriction}\kern 20.00003pt&\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}\hfil\kern 10.00002pt&P\mathbin{|}Q&\text{parallel}\\
~~\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}&\bm{0}&\text{inaction}\kern 20.00003pt&\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}\hfil\kern 10.00002pt&[x\leftrightarrow y]&\text{forwarder}\\
~~\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}&\mu X(\tilde{z});P&\text{recursive definition}\kern 20.00003pt&\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}\hfil\kern 10.00002pt&X\langle\tilde{z}\rangle&\text{recursive call}\end{array}

Figure

2
(top) gives the syntax of processes.
The send
x[a,b]
sends along
x
a message name
a
and a continuation name
b
.
The receive
x(y,z);P
blocks until on
x
a message and continuation name are received (referred to in
P
as the placeholders
y
and
z
, respectively), binding
y
and
z
in
P
.
The selection
x[b]\triangleleft i
sends along
x
a label
i
and a continuation name
b
.
The branch
x(z)\triangleright\{i:P_{i}\}_{i\in I}
blocks until it receives on
x
a label
i\in I
and a continuation name (referred to in
P_{i}
as the placeholder
z
), binding
z
in each
P_{i}
.
In the rest of this paper, we refer to sends, receives, selections, and branches—including their continuations, if any—as
prefixes
.
We refer to sends and selections collectively as
outputs
, and to receives and branches as
inputs
.

Restriction
(\bm{\nu}xy)P
binds
x
and
y
in
P
, thus declaring them as the two names of a channel and enabling communication, as in
[
Vas12
]
.
The process
P\mathbin{|}Q
denotes the parallel composition of
P
and
Q
.
The process
\bm{0}
denotes inaction.
The forwarder
[x\leftrightarrow y]
is a primitive copycat process that links together
x
and
y
.
We say a forwarder
[x\leftrightarrow y]
in
P
is
independent
if
P
does not bind
x
and
y
together through restriction (and
dependent
if it does).
The process
\mu X(\tilde{z});P
denotes a recursive definition, binding occurrences of
X
in
P
; the names
\tilde{z}
form a context for
P
.
Then
P
may contain recursive calls
X\langle\tilde{z}\rangle
that indicate a repetition of
P
, providing the names
\tilde{z}
as context.
We only consider contractive recursion, disallowing processes with subexpressions of the form
\mu X_{1}(\tilde{z});\ldots;\mu X_{n}(\tilde{z});X_{1}\langle\tilde{z}\rangle
.

Names and recursion variables
are free unless otherwise stated (i.e., unless they are bound somehow).
We write
\fn(P)

and
\frv(P)

for the sets of free names
and free recursion variables
of
P
, respectively,
and
\bn(P)
for the set of bound names of
P
.
Also, we write
P\{x/y\}
to denote the capture-avoiding substitution of the free occurrences of
y
in
P
for
x
.
Notation
P\big\{\big(\mu X(y_{1},\ldots,y_{n});P^{\prime}\big)/X\langle y_{1},\ldots,y_{n}\rangle\big\}
denotes the substitution of occurrences of recursive calls
X\langle y_{1},\ldots,y_{n}\rangle
in
P
with the recursive definition
\mu X(y_{1},\ldots,y_{n});P^{\prime}
, which we call
unfolding
recursion.
We write sequences of substitutions
P\{x_{1}/y_{1}\}\ldots\{x_{n}/y_{n}\}
as
P\{x_{1}/y_{1},\ldots,x_{n}/y_{n}\}
.

Except for asynchrony and recursion, there are minor differences with respect to the languages of Dardha and Gay
[
DG18
]
and DeYoung
et al.

[
DCPT12
]
.
Unlike Dardha and Gay’s, our syntax does not include empty send and receive prefixes that explicitly close channels; this simplifies the type system.
We also do not include the operator for replicated servers, denoted
{!}x(y);P
, which is present in
[
DG18
,
DCPT12
]
.
Although replication can be handled without difficulties, we omit it here; we prefer focusing on recursion, because it fits well with the examples we consider.
See
Section

3.6
for further discussion.

A Convenient Notation

In the send
x[a,b]
and the selection

x[b]\triangleleft\ell
, names
a
and
b
are
free
. They can be bound to a continuation process using parallel composition and restriction, as in
(\bm{\nu}ay)(\bm{\nu}bz)(x[a,b]\mathbin{|}P_{y,z})

and

(\bm{\nu}bz)(x[b]\triangleleft\ell]\mathbin{|}Q_{z})
,
respectively.
We introduce useful notations that abstract away from elide these constructs and continuation names:

Notation
\thethm

(Derivable Bound Communication)
.

We use the following syntactic sugar:

\displaystyle\overline{x}[y]\cdot P

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}(\bm{\nu}ya)(\bm{\nu}zb)(x[a,b]\mathbin{|}P\{z/x\})

\displaystyle\overline{x}\triangleleft\ell\cdot P

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}(\bm{\nu}zb)(x[b]\triangleleft\ell\mathbin{|}P\{z/x\})

\displaystyle x(y);P

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}x(y,z);P\{z/x\}

\displaystyle x\triangleright\{i:P_{i}\}_{i\in I}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}x(z)\triangleright\{i:P_{i}\{z/x\}\}_{i\in I}

Note our use of ‘
\,\cdot\,
’ instead of ‘
\,;\,
’ in sending and selection to stress that they are non-blocking operators.
As we will see, these derived constructs are typable (
2
)

Operational Semantics

We define a reduction relation for processes (
P\mathbin{\rightarrow}Q
) that formalizes how complementary outputs/inputs on connected names may synchronize.
As usual for
\pi
-calculi, reduction relies on
structural congruence
(
P\equiv Q
), which relates processes with minor syntactic differences.
Structural congruence is the smallest congruence on the syntax of processes (
Figure

2
(top)) satisfying the axioms in
Figure

2
(center).

{defi}

[Structural Congruence (
\equiv
) for
APCP
]

Structural congruence for
APCP
, denoted
P\equiv Q
, is the smallest congruence on the syntax of processes (
Section

3.1
) satisfying the axioms in
Figure

3
.

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{cong-alpha}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$P\equiv_{\alpha}Q$}\hskip 4.0pt}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to39.96886pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 1.46053pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$P\equiv Q$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{cong-par-unit}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 17.35066pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to42.70134pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$P\mathbin{|}\bm{0}\equiv P$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{cong-par-comm}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 25.15898pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to58.31796pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$P\mathbin{|}Q\equiv Q\mathbin{|}P$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{cong-par-assoc}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 43.38472pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to94.76944pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$P\mathbin{|}(Q\mathbin{|}R)\equiv(P\mathbin{|}Q)\mathbin{|}R$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{cong-scope}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 26.11215pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$x,y\notin\fn(P)$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to113.48474pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$P\mathbin{|}(\bm{\nu}xy)Q\equiv(\bm{\nu}xy)(P\mathbin{|}Q)$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{cong-res-comm}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 63.40631pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to134.81262pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$(\bm{\nu}xy)(\bm{\nu}zw)P\equiv(\bm{\nu}zw)(\bm{\nu}xy)P$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{cong-res-symm}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 38.17014pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to84.34027pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$(\bm{\nu}xy)P\equiv(\bm{\nu}yx)P$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{cong-res-inact}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 21.48611pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to50.97223pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$(\bm{\nu}xy)\bm{0}\equiv\bm{0}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{cong-fwd-symm}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 38.75449pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to85.50897pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$[x\leftrightarrow y]\equiv[y\leftrightarrow x]$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{cong-res-fwd}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 36.04391pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to80.08784pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$(\bm{\nu}xy)[x\leftrightarrow y]\equiv\bm{0}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{cong-unfold}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 161.00697pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to330.01396pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mu X(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n});P\equiv P\big\{\big(\mu X(y_{1},\ldots,y_{n});P\{y_{1}/x_{1},\ldots,y_{n}/x_{n}\}\big)/X\langle y_{1},\ldots,y_{n}\rangle\big\}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}

Figure 3.
Structural congruence for
APCP
: axioms.

Structural congruence defines the following properties for processes.
Processes are equivalent up to
\alpha
equivalence (Rule [
cong-alpha
]).
Parallel composition is associative (Rule [
cong-par-assoc
]) and commutative (Rule [
cong-par-comm
]), with unit
\bm{0}
(Rule [
cong-par-unit
]).
A parallel process may be moved into or out of a restriction as long as the bound channels do not occur free in the moved process (Rule [
cong-scope
]): this is
scope inclusion
and
scope extrusion
, respectively.
Restrictions on inactive processes may be dropped (Rule [
cong-res-inact
]), and the order of names in restrictions and of consecutive restrictions does not matter (Rules [
cong-res-symm
] and [
cong-res-comm
], respectively).
Forwarders are symmetric (Rule [
cong-fwd-symm
]), and equivalent to inaction if both names are bound together through restriction (Rule [
cong-res-fwd
]).
Finally, a recursive definition is equivalent to its unfolding (Rule [
cong-unfold
]), replacing any recursive calls with copies of the recursive definition, where the recursive definition’s contextual names are pairwise substituted for by the call’s names.

As we will see next, the semantics of
APCP
is closed under structural congruence.
This means that processes are
equi-recursive
; however,
APCP
’s typing discipline (described in
Section

3.2
) treats recursive types as
iso-recursive
(see, e.g., Pierce
[
Pie02
]
).

We define the reduction relation
P\mathbin{\rightarrow}Q
by the axioms and closure rules in
Figure

2
(bottom).
We write
\mathbin{\rightarrow}^{\ast}
for the reflexive, transitive closure of
\mathbin{\rightarrow}
.
Rule
{{[\textsc{red-send-recv}]}}
synchronizes a send and a receive on connected names and substitutes the message and continuation names.
Rule
{{[\textsc{red-sel-bra}]}}
synchronizes a selection and a branch:
the received label determines the continuation process, substituting the continuation name appropriately.
Rule
{{[\textsc{red-fwd}]}}
implements the forwarder as a substitution.
Rules
{{[\textsc{red-cong}]}}
,
{{[\textsc{red-res}]}}
, and
{{[\textsc{red-par}]}}
close reduction under structural congruence, restriction, and parallel composition, respectively.

Having communication of free names in sends and selections is different from communication in the works by Dardha and Gay
[
DG18
]
and DeYoung
et al.

[
DCPT12
]
, where, following an internal mobility discipline
[
Bor98
]
, communication involves bound names only.
Still, notice that free output is expressible in those works by combining bound output and forwarding.

{exa}

To illustrate the preservation of order
within
a session and the asynchrony
between
different sessions, we consider the following process:

P\mathrel{\triangleq}(\bm{\nu}zu)\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}\big((\bm{\nu}xy)\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}\big((\bm{\nu}ax^{\prime})(x[v_{1},a]\mathbin{|}x^{\prime}[v_{2},b])\\
{}\mathbin{|}(\bm{\nu}cz^{\prime})(z[v_{3},c]\mathbin{|}y(w_{1},y^{\prime});y^{\prime}(w_{2},y^{\prime\prime});Q)\big)\end{array}\\
{}\mathbin{|}u(w_{3},u^{\prime});R\big)\end{array}

The process
P
defines two consecutive sends on a session from
x
to
y
, and an asynchronous send on a session from
z
to
u
.
Two reductions are possible from
P
:

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}(\bm{\nu}zu)\big((\bm{\nu}ax^{\prime})\big(x^{\prime}[v_{2},b]\mathbin{|}(\bm{\nu}cz^{\prime})(z[v_{3},c]\mathbin{|}a(w_{2},y^{\prime\prime});Q\{v_{1}/w_{1}\})\big)\mathbin{|}u(w_{3},u^{\prime});R\big)

(1)

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}(\bm{\nu}cz^{\prime})\big((\bm{\nu}xy)\big((\bm{\nu}ax^{\prime})(x[v_{1},a]\mathbin{|}x^{\prime}[v_{2},b])\mathbin{|}y(w_{1},y^{\prime});y^{\prime}(w_{2},y^{\prime\prime});Q\big)\mathbin{|}R\{v_{3}/w_{3},c/u^{\prime}\}\big)

(2)

The reduction (
1
) entails the synchronization of the send on
x
and the receive on
y
; afterwards, the send on
x^{\prime}
is connected to the receive on
a
that prefixes
Q
.
The reduction (
2
) entails the synchronization of the send on
z
and the receive on
u
, connecting
Q
and
R
on a new session between
z^{\prime}
and
c
.
Note that from
P
there is no reduction involving the send on
x^{\prime}
, since
x^{\prime}
is connected to the continuation name of the send on
x
and is thus not (yet) paired with a dual receive.

Using the sugared syntax from
Section

3.1
, we can write

P=(\bm{\nu}zu)((\bm{\nu}xy)(\overline{x}[v_{1}]\cdot\overline{x}[v_{2}]\cdot\bm{0}\mathbin{|}\overline{z}[v_{3}]\cdot y(w_{1});y(w_{2});Q^{\prime})\mathbin{|}u(w_{3});R^{\prime})

where
Q^{\prime}\mathrel{\triangleq}Q\{y/y^{\prime\prime}\}
and
R^{\prime}\mathrel{\triangleq}R\{u/u^{\prime}\}
.

The following diagram illustrates all the possible reduction paths from
P
; horizontal reductions concern the session between
x
and
y
, and diagonal reductions concern the session between
z
and
u
:

\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{\kern 1pt}l@{}l@{\kern 1pt}l@{}l@{\kern 1pt}l@{}l@{}}P\hfil\kern 1.0&\mathbin{\rightarrow}&P_{1}\hfil\kern 1.0&\mathbin{\rightarrow}&P_{3}\hfil\kern 1.0\\[-5.0pt]
\hfil\kern 1.0&\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\mathbin{\rightarrow}$}&\hfil\kern 1.0&\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\mathbin{\rightarrow}$}&\hfil\kern 1.0&\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\mathbin{\rightarrow}$}\\[-5.0pt]
\hfil\kern 1.0&&P_{2}\hfil\kern 1.0&\mathbin{\rightarrow}&P_{4}\hfil\kern 1.0&\mathbin{\rightarrow}&P_{5}\end{array}

where processes
P_{1}
,
P_{2}
,
P_{3}
,
P_{4}
, and
P_{5}
are as follows:

\displaystyle P_{1}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}(\bm{\nu}zu)((\bm{\nu}xy)(\overline{x}[v_{2}]\cdot\bm{0}\mathbin{|}\overline{z}[v_{3}]\cdot y(w_{2});Q^{\prime}\{v_{1}/w_{1}\})\mathbin{|}u(w_{3});R^{\prime})

\displaystyle P_{2}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}(\bm{\nu}xy)(\overline{x}[v_{1}]\cdot\overline{x}[v_{2}]\cdot\bm{0}\mathbin{|}y(w_{1});y(w_{2});Q^{\prime})\mathbin{|}R^{\prime}\{v_{3}/w_{3}\}

\displaystyle P_{3}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}(\bm{\nu}zu)(\overline{z}[v_{3}]\cdot Q^{\prime}\{v_{1}/w_{1},v_{2}/w_{2}\}\mathbin{|}u(w_{3});R^{\prime})

\displaystyle P_{4}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}(\bm{\nu}xy)(\overline{x}[v_{2}]\cdot\bm{0}\mathbin{|}y(w_{2});Q^{\prime}\{v_{1}/w_{1}\})\mathbin{|}R^{\prime}\{v_{3}/w_{3}\}

\displaystyle P_{5}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}Q^{\prime}\{v_{1}/w_{1},v_{2}/w_{2}\}\mathbin{|}R^{\prime}\{v_{3}/w_{3}\}

Presentations of reduction for session-typed
\pi
-calculi derived from the Curry-Howard interpretations of linear logic often include rules that correspond to
commuting conversions
in linear logic (cf.
[
CP10
,
Wad12
,
DG18
,
DCPT12
]
), which allow rewriting processes in such a way that blocking prefixes on free names are “pulled out” of restrictions.
Commuting conversions can be easily included for
APCP
(cf.
[
HP21
]
), but we do not consider them here.
While Dardha and Gay
[
DG18
]
rely on commuting conversions to prove deadlock freedom, the proof of deadlock freedom for
APCP
takes a different approach and does not require commuting conversions—see
Section

3.3
for a detailed discussion.

3.2.
The Type System

APCP
types processes by assigning binary session types to names.
Following Curry-Howard interpretations, we present session types as linear logic propositions (cf., e.g., Caires
et al.

[
CPT16
]
, Wadler
[
Wad12
]
, Caires and Pérez
[
CP17
]
, and Dardha and Gay
[
DG18
]
).
We extend these propositions with recursion and
priority
annotations on connectives.
Intuitively, prefixes typed with lower priority should not be blocked by those with higher priority.

We write
\circ,\pi,\rho,\ldots
to denote priorities, and
\omega
to denote the ultimate priority that is greater than all other priorities and cannot be increased further.
That is,
\forall\circ\in\mathbb{N}.~\omega>\circ
and
\forall\circ\in\mathbb{N}.~\omega+\circ=\omega
.

{defi}

[Session Types for
APCP
]

The following grammar defines the syntax of
session types

A,B
.
Let
\circ\in\mathbb{N}
.

\displaystyle A,B

\displaystyle::=A\mathbin{\otimes}^{\circ}B\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{\circ}B\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}{\oplus}^{\circ}\{i:A\}_{i\in I}\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}\&^{\circ}\{i:A\}_{i\in I}\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}\bullet\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}\mu X.A\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}X

A name of type
A\mathbin{\otimes}^{\circ}B
(resp.
A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{\circ}B
) first sends (resp. receives) a name of type
A
and then behaves as
B
.
A name of type
{\oplus}^{\circ}\{i:A_{i}\}_{i\in I}
selects a label
i\in I
and then behaves as
A_{i}
.
A name of type
\&^{\circ}\{i:A_{i}\}_{i\in I}
offers a choice: after receiving a label
i\in I
, the name behaves as
A_{i}
.
A name of type
\bullet
is closed; it does not require a priority, as closed names do not exhibit behavior and thus are non-blocking.

Unlike Caires and Pfenning
[
CP10
]
and Dardha and Gay
[
DG18
]
,
APCP
does not associate any behavior with closed sessions (i.e., no closing synchronizations).
Moreover, as we will see,
APCP
’s type system allows arbitrary parallel composition.
Atkey
et al.

[
ALM16
]
have shown that, in presence of arbitrary parallel composition (i.e., linear logic’s Rule [
mix
] that combines sequents arbitrarily, usually interpreted as session typing rule for arbitrary parallel composition), the dual propositions
\bm{1}
and
\bot
(usually associated with complementary prefixes for closing sessions) are equivalent.
Hence, since in
APCP
session closing is silent, we follow Caires
[
Cai14
]
in conflating the types
\bm{1}
and
\bot
to the single, self-dual type
\bullet
for closed sessions.

Type
\mu X.A
denotes a recursive type, in which
A
may contain occurrences of the recursion variable
X
.
As customary,
\mu
is a binder: it induces the standard notions of
\alpha
equivalence, substitution (denoted
A\{B/X\}
), and free recursion variables (denoted
\frv(A)
).
We work with tail-recursive, contractive types, disallowing types of the form
\mu X_{1}\ldots\mu X_{n}.X_{1}
and
\mu X.X\mathbin{\otimes}^{\circ}A
.
Recursive types are treated iso-recursively: there will be an explicit typing rule that unfolds recursive types, and recursive types are not equal to their unfolding.
We postpone formalizing the unfolding of recursive types, as it requires additional definitions to ensure consistency of priorities upon unfolding.

Duality
, the cornerstone notion of session types and linear logic, ensures that the two names of a channel have
complementary behaviors.
Furthermore, dual types must have matching priority annotations.
The following inductive definition of duality suffices for our tail-recursive types (cf. Gay
et al.

[
GTV20
]
).

{defi}

[Duality]

The
dual
of session type
A
, denoted
\overline{A}
, is defined inductively as follows:

\displaystyle\overline{A\mathbin{\otimes}^{\circ}B}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}\overline{A}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{\circ}\overline{B}

\displaystyle\overline{{\oplus}^{\circ}\{i:A_{i}\}_{i\in I}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}\&^{\circ}\{i:\overline{A_{i}}\}_{i\in I}

\displaystyle\overline{\bullet}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}\bullet

\displaystyle\overline{\mu X.A}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}\mu X.\overline{A}

\displaystyle\overline{A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{\circ}B}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}\overline{A}\mathbin{\otimes}^{\circ}\overline{B}

\displaystyle\overline{\&^{\circ}\{i:A_{i}\}_{i\in I}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}{\oplus}^{\circ}\{i:\overline{A_{i}}\}_{i\in I}

\displaystyle\overline{X}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}X

The priority of a type is determined by the priority of the type’s outermost connective:

{defi}

[Priorities]

For session type
A
,
\pr(A)
denotes its
priority
:

\displaystyle\pr(A\mathbin{\otimes}^{\circ}B)\mathrel{\triangleq}\pr(A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{\circ}B)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}\circ

\displaystyle\pr(\mu X.A)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}\pr(A)

\displaystyle\pr({\oplus}^{\circ}\{i:A_{i}\}_{i\in I})\mathrel{\triangleq}\pr(\&^{\circ}\{i:A_{i}\}_{i\in I})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}\circ

\displaystyle\pr(\bullet)\mathrel{\triangleq}\pr(X)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}\omega

The priority of
\bullet
and
X
is the constant
\omega
: they denote the “final”, non-blocking part of protocols.
Although
\mathbin{\otimes}
and
{\oplus}
also denote non-blocking prefixes, they do block their continuation until they are received.
Hence, their priority is not constant.

We now turn to formalizing the unfolding of recursive types.
Recall the intuition that prefixes typed with lower priority should not be blocked by those with higher priority.
Based on this rationale, we observe that the unfolding of the recursive type
\mu X.A
should not result in
A\{(\mu X.A)/X\}
, as usual; rather, the priorities of the unfolded type should be
increased
to ensure a global ordering between actions.

{exa}

Consider the recursive type
\mu X.A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{0}X
.
By unfolding this type without increasing the priority, we obtain
A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{0}(\mu X.A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{0}X)
, a type in which the priorities do not ensure a global ordering between the two receives.
In contrast, increasing the priority in the unfolded type as in, e.g.,
A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{0}(\mu X.A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{1}X)
, does ensure a global ordering.

We make this intuition precise by defining the
lift
of priorities in types:

{defi}

[Lift]

For proposition
A
and
t\in\mathbb{N}
, we define
{\uparrow^{\mkern-3.0mut}}A
as the
lift
operation:

\displaystyle{\uparrow^{\mkern-3.0mut}}(A\mathbin{\otimes}^{\circ}B)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}({\uparrow^{\mkern-3.0mut}}A)\mathbin{\otimes}^{\circ+t}({\uparrow^{\mkern-3.0mut}}B)

\displaystyle{\uparrow^{\mkern-3.0mut}}({\oplus}^{\circ}\{i:A_{i}\}_{i\in I})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}{\oplus}^{\circ+t}\{i:{\uparrow^{\mkern-3.0mut}}A_{i}\}_{i\in I}

\displaystyle{\uparrow^{\mkern-3.0mut}}\bullet

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}\bullet

\displaystyle{\uparrow^{\mkern-3.0mut}}(A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{\circ}B)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}({\uparrow^{\mkern-3.0mut}}A)\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{\circ+t}({\uparrow^{\mkern-3.0mut}}B)

\displaystyle{\uparrow^{\mkern-3.0mut}}(\&^{\circ}\{i:A_{i}\}_{i\in I})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}\&^{\circ+t}\{i:{\uparrow^{\mkern-3.0mut}}A_{i}\}_{i\in I}

\displaystyle{\uparrow^{\mkern-3.0mut}}(\mu X.A)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}\mu X.({\uparrow^{\mkern-3.0mut}}A)

\displaystyle{\uparrow^{\mkern-3.0mut}}X

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}X

Henceforth, the unfolding of
\mu X.A
is
A\{\big(\mu X.({\uparrow^{\mkern-3.0mut}}A)\big)/X\}
, denoted
\unfold^{t}(\mu X.A)
, where
t\in\mathbb{N}
depends on the highest priority of the types occurring in a typing context.
We recall that we do not consider types to be equi-recursive: recursive types are not equal to their unfolding.
Recursive types can only be unfolded by typing rules, discussed next.

We now define the top priority of a type, i.e., the highest priority appearing in a type:

{defi}

[Top Priority]

For session type
A
,
\prtop(A)
denotes its
top priority
:

\displaystyle\prtop(A\mathbin{\otimes}^{\circ}B)\mathrel{\triangleq}\prtop(A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{\circ}B)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}{\mathchoice{\textstyle}{}{}{}\max}(\prtop(A),\prtop(B),\circ)

\displaystyle\prtop({\oplus}^{\circ}\{i:A_{i}\}_{i\in I})\mathrel{\triangleq}\prtop(\&^{\circ}\{i:A_{i}\}_{i\in I})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}{\mathchoice{\textstyle}{}{}{}\max}({\mathchoice{\textstyle}{}{}{}\max}_{i\in I}(\prtop(A_{i})),\circ)

\displaystyle\prtop(\mu X.A)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}\prtop(A)

\displaystyle\prtop(\bullet)\mathrel{\triangleq}\prtop(X)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}0

Notice how the top priority of
\bullet
and
X
is 0, in contrast to their priority (as given by
Section

3.2
): as we will see next, they do not contribute to the increase in priority needed for unfolding recursive types.

Typing Rules

The typing rules of
APCP
ensure that prefixes with lower priority are not blocked by those with higher priority (cf. Dardha and Gay
[
DG18
]
).
To this end, they enforce the following laws:

(1)

Sends and selections with priority
\circ
must have continuations/payloads with priority strictly larger than
\circ
;

(2)

A prefix with priority
\circ
must be prefixed only by receives and branches with priority strictly smaller than
\circ
;

(3)

Dual prefixes leading to a synchronization must have equal priorities (cf.
Section

3.2
).

Judgments are of the form
\Omega\vdash P::\Gamma
, where:

•

P
is a process;

•

\Gamma
is a context that assigns types to channels (
x:A
);

•

\Omega
is a context that assigns tuples of types to recursion variables (
X:(A,B,\ldots)
).

A judgment
\Omega\vdash P::\Gamma
then means that
P
can be typed in accordance with the type assignments for names recorded in
\Gamma
and the recursion variables in
\Omega
.
Intuitively, the latter context ensures that types concur between the context names of recursive definitions and calls.
Both contexts
\Gamma
and
\Omega
obey
exchange
: assignments may be silently reordered.

\Gamma
is
linear
, disallowing
weakening
(i.e., all assignments must be used, except names typed
\bullet
) and
contraction
(i.e., assignments may not be duplicated).

\Omega
allows weakening and contraction, because a recursive definition may be called
zero or more
times.

The empty context is written
\emptyset
.
In writing
\Gamma,x:A
we assume that
x\notin\dom(\Gamma)
(and similarly for
\Omega
).
We write
{\uparrow^{\mkern-3.0mut}}\Gamma
to denote the component-wise extension of lift (
Section

3.2
) to typing contexts.
Also, we write
\pr(\Gamma)
to denote the least of the priorities of all types in
\Gamma
(
Section

3.2
).
An assignment
\tilde{z}:\tilde{A}
means
z_{1}:A_{1},\ldots,z_{k}:A_{k}
.
We define the top priority of a sequence of types
\prtop(\tilde{A})
as
{\mathchoice{\textstyle}{}{}{}\max}_{A_{i}\in\tilde{A}}(\prtop(A_{i}))
.

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-send}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 36.04486pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\circ<\pr(A),\pr(B)$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to146.42899pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega\vdash x[y,z]::x:A\mathbin{\otimes}^{\circ}B,y:\overline{A},z:\overline{B}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-recv}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 41.52968pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega\vdash P::\Gamma,y:A,z:B$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\circ<\pr(\Gamma)$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to245.69214pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega\vdash x(y,z);P::\Gamma,x:A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{\circ}B$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-sel}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 32.89345pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$j\in I$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\circ<\pr(A_{j})$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to160.42361pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega\vdash x[z]\triangleleft j::x:{\oplus}^{\circ}\{i:A_{i}\}_{i\in I},z:\overline{A_{j}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-bra}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 7.35872pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\forall i\in I.~\Omega\vdash P_{i}::\Gamma,z:A_{i}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\circ<\pr(\Gamma)$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to189.28276pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega\vdash x(z)\triangleright\{i:P_{i}\}_{i\in I}::\Gamma,x:\&^{\circ}\{i:A_{i}\}_{i\in I}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-end}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 10.35759pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega\vdash P::\Gamma$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to72.774pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega\vdash P::\Gamma,x:\bullet$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-par}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega\vdash P::\Gamma$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega\vdash Q::\Delta$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to120.75124pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 22.61566pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega\vdash P\mathbin{|}Q::\Gamma,\Delta$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-res}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega\vdash P::\Gamma,x:A,y:\overline{A}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to98.31323pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 11.27997pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega\vdash(\bm{\nu}xy)P::\Gamma$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-inact}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 17.59715pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to43.19432pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega\vdash\bm{0}::\emptyset$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-fwd}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 51.94882pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to111.89764pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega\vdash[x\leftrightarrow y]::x:\overline{A},y:A$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-rec}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega,X:{\tilde{A}}\vdash P::\tilde{z}:\tilde{U}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$t\in\mathbb{N}>\prtop(\tilde{A})$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\forall U_{i}\in\tilde{U}.~U_{i}=\unfold^{t}(\mu X.A_{i})$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to306.6992pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 108.45325pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega\vdash\mu X(\tilde{z});P::\tilde{z}:\widetilde{\mu X.A}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-var}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$t\in\mathbb{N}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\forall U_{i}\in\tilde{U}.~U_{i}=\mu X.{\uparrow^{\mkern-3.0mut}}A_{i}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to144.61227pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 27.01451pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega,X:\tilde{A}\vdash X\langle\tilde{z}\rangle::\tilde{z}:\tilde{U}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}\\
\vskip-5.0pt\hbox to345.0pt{\leaders{\hbox to3.0pt{\hss. \hss}}{\hfil}}\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\\
\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-send$\star$}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega\vdash P::\Gamma,y:A,x:B$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\circ<\pr(A),\pr(B)$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to192.97076pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 37.09781pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega\vdash\overline{x}[y]\cdot P::\Gamma,x:A\mathbin{\otimes}^{\circ}B$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-sel$\star$}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega\vdash P::\Gamma,x:A_{j}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$j\in I$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\circ<\pr(A_{j})$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to191.35576pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 20.82889pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega\vdash\overline{x}\triangleleft j\cdot P::\Gamma,x:{\oplus}^{\circ}\{i:A_{i}\}_{i\in I}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-lift}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega\vdash P::\Gamma$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$t\in\mathbb{N}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to95.0681pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 19.46638pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega\vdash P::{\uparrow^{\mkern-3.0mut}}\Gamma$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}

Figure 4.
The typing rules of
APCP
(top) and derivable rules (bottom).

Figure

4
(top) gives the typing rules.
We describe the typing rules from a
bottom-up
perspective.
Rule [
typ-send
] types a send; this rule does not have premises to provide a continuation process, leaving the free names to be bound to a continuation process using Rules [
typ-par
] and [
typ-res
] (discussed hereafter).
Similarly, Rule [
typ-sel
] types an unbound selection.
Both rules require that the priority of the subject is lower than the priorities of both objects (continuation and payload)—this enforces Law
1
.
Rules [
typ-recv
] and [
typ-bra
] type receives and branches, respectively.
In both cases, the used name’s priority must be lower than the priorities of the other types in the continuation’s typing context—this enforces Law
2
.

Rule [
typ-par
] types the parallel composition of two processes that do not share assignments on the same names.
Rule [
typ-res
] types a restriction, where the two restricted names must be of dual type and thus have matching priority—this enforces Law
3
.
Rule [
typ-end
] silently removes a closed name from the typing context.
Rule [
typ-inact
] types an inactive process with no names.
Rule [
typ-fwd
] types forwarding between names of dual type—this also enforces Law
3
.

{exa}

To illustrate the typing rules discussed so far, we recall process
P
from
Section

3.1
:

P=(\bm{\nu}zu)\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}\big((\bm{\nu}xy)\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}\big((\bm{\nu}ax^{\prime})(x[v_{1},a]\mathbin{|}x^{\prime}[v_{2},b])\\
{}\mathbin{|}(\bm{\nu}cz^{\prime})(z[v_{3},c]\mathbin{|}y(w_{1},y^{\prime});y^{\prime}(w_{2},y^{\prime\prime});Q)\big)\end{array}\\
{}\mathbin{|}u(w_{3},u^{\prime});R\big)\end{array}

We give the typing of the two consecutive sends on
x
(omitting the context
\Omega
):

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 36.33926pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\circ<\pr(A_{1}),\pi$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to133.1856pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{typ-send}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash x[v_{1},a]::\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}x:A_{1}\mathbin{\otimes}^{\circ}A_{2}\mathbin{\otimes}^{\pi}B,\\
v_{1}:\overline{A_{1}},a:\overline{A_{2}\mathbin{\otimes}^{\pi}B}\end{array}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 15.56897pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\pi<\pr(A_{2}),\pr(B)$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to113.4413pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{typ-send}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash x^{\prime}[v_{2},b]::\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}x^{\prime}:A_{2}\mathbin{\otimes}^{\pi}B,\\
v_{2}:\overline{A_{2}},b:\overline{B}\end{array}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to298.38777pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{typ-par}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 37.28854pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash x[v_{1},a]\mathbin{|}x^{\prime}[v_{2},b]::\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}v_{1}:\overline{A_{1}},v_{2}:\overline{A_{2}},b:\overline{B},x:A_{1}\mathbin{\otimes}^{\circ}A_{2}\mathbin{\otimes}^{\pi}B,\\
a:\overline{A_{2}\mathbin{\otimes}^{\pi}B},x^{\prime}:A_{2}\mathbin{\otimes}^{\pi}B\end{array}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 17.77657pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to262.83464pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{typ-res}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 17.77657pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash(\bm{\nu}ax^{\prime})(x[v_{1},a]\mathbin{|}x^{\prime}[v_{2},b])::v_{1}:\overline{A_{1}},v_{2}:\overline{A_{2}},b:\overline{B},x:A_{1}\mathbin{\otimes}^{\circ}A_{2}\mathbin{\otimes}^{\pi}B$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}

As discussed before, this typing leaves the (free) names
v_{1}
,
v_{2}
, and
b
to be accounted for by the context.

Now let us derive the typing of the consecutive receives on
y
, i.e., of the subprocess
y(w_{1},y^{\prime});y^{\prime}(w_{2},y^{\prime\prime});Q
.
Because
x
and
y
are dual names in
P
, the type of
y
should be dual to the type of
x
above:

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 27.14873pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 33.14153pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash Q::\Gamma,w_{1}:\overline{A_{1}},w_{2}:\overline{A_{2}},y^{\prime\prime}:\overline{B}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\pi<\pr(\Gamma,w_{1}:\overline{A_{1}})$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to266.00114pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{typ-recv}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash y^{\prime}(w_{2},y^{\prime\prime});Q::\Gamma,w_{1}:\overline{A_{1}},y^{\prime}:\overline{A_{2}}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{\pi}\overline{B}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\circ<\pr(\Gamma)$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to415.5663pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{typ-recv}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash y(w_{1},y^{\prime});y^{\prime}(w_{2},y^{\prime\prime});Q::\Gamma,y:\overline{A_{1}}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{\circ}\overline{A_{2}}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{\pi}\overline{B}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}

These two derivations tell us that
\circ<\pi<\pr(A_{1}),\pr(A_{2}),\pr(B),\pr(\Gamma)
.
This way, the type system ensures that none of the sessions in
Q
can be connected to sessions that block the sends on
x,x^{\prime}
, which may leave the door open for a deadlock otherwise.
In
Section

5.3
,
Section

5.3
illustrates such a situation in the context of
\texttt{LAST}^{\mathit{n}}
and how the type system catches it.

Consider the usual Rule [
typ-cut
] in type systems based on linear logic
[
CP10
,
Wad12
]
:

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-cut}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega\vdash P::\Gamma,x:A$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega\vdash Q::\Delta,y:\overline{A}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to167.00566pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 30.00673pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega\vdash(\bm{\nu}xy)(P\mathbin{|}Q)::\Gamma,\Delta$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}

Note that a single application of Rule [
typ-par
] followed by Rule [
typ-res
] coincides with Rule [
typ-cut
].
Without annotations and conditions related to priority, Rules [
typ-par
] and [
typ-res
] give rise to deadlocks, as the following example shows.

{exa}

Consider the following process, arguably the paradigmatic example of a deadlock:

Q\mathrel{\triangleq}(\bm{\nu}xy)(\bm{\nu}zw)(x(u);\overline{z}[u^{\prime}]\cdot\bm{0}\mathbin{|}w(v);\overline{y}[v^{\prime}]\cdot\bm{0})

Without priorities (and priority checks), this process can be typed using Rules [
typ-par
] and [
typ-res
] (omitting “
typ-
” from rule labels):

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 65.63562pt\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 8.05557pt\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 36.41893pt\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 12.59718pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to33.19438pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{inact}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash\bm{0}::\emptyset$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip-0.46092pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-1.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to106.03223pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\hss}\raise 2.0pt\hbox to106.03223pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}}\lower 0.71092pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize${{[\textsc{end}]}}^{4}$}}\vskip-0.46092pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash\bm{0}::z:\bullet,x:\bullet,u:\bullet,u^{\prime}:\bullet$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to122.14337pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{send$\star$}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash\overline{z}[u^{\prime}]\cdot\bm{0}::z:\bullet\mathbin{\otimes}\bullet,x:\bullet,u:\bullet$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to253.41463pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{recv}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash x(u);\overline{z}[u^{\prime}]\cdot\bm{0}::z:\bullet\mathbin{\otimes}\bullet,x:\bullet\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}\bullet$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\hbox{\hskip 4.30554pt}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 66.49211pt\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 8.05557pt\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 36.8425pt\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 12.59718pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to33.19438pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{inact}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash\bm{0}::\emptyset$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip-0.46092pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-1.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to106.87936pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\hss}\raise 2.0pt\hbox to106.87936pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}}\lower 0.71092pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize${{[\textsc{end}]}}^{4}$}}\vskip-0.46092pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash\bm{0}::w:\bullet,y:\bullet,v:\bullet,v^{\prime}:\bullet$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to122.99051pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{send$\star$}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash\overline{y}[v^{\prime}]\cdot\bm{0}::w:\bullet,y:\bullet\mathbin{\otimes}\bullet,v:\bullet$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to255.97475pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{recv}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash w(v);\overline{y}[v^{\prime}]\cdot\bm{0}::w:\bullet\mathbin{\otimes}\bullet,y:\bullet\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}\bullet$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip-0.46092pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-1.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to535.6603pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\hss}\raise 2.0pt\hbox to535.6603pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}}\lower 0.71092pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{par}]}}+${{[\textsc{res}]}}^{2}$}}\vskip-0.46092pt\hbox{\hskip 247.37746pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash Q::\emptyset$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}

On the other hand, were we to restrict parallel composition and restriction using Rule [
typ-cut
],
Q
would not be typable: Rule [
typ-cut
] can only type one of the restrictions, not both.
With priorities,
Q
would not be typable either, due to the requirements induced by Rule [
typ-recv
]: (i) the priority
\circ
of the input on
x
is smaller than the priority
\pi
of the send on
z
(left-hand side above), and (ii) the priority
\pi
of the input on
w
is smaller than the priority
\circ
of the send on
y
(right-hand side above).
Clearly, these requirements combined are unsatisfiable.

Rules [
typ-rec
] and [
typ-var
] type recursive definitions and recursive calls, respectively.
To justify their formulation, let us consider naive formulations for each of them:

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-rec-naive}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 30.13773pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega\vdash P::\tilde{z}:\tilde{A}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to117.19548pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega,X:|\tilde{z}|\vdash\mu X(\tilde{z});P::\tilde{z}:\widetilde{\mu X.A}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-var-naive}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 44.06941pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to96.13884pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Omega,X:|\tilde{z}|\vdash X\langle\tilde{z}\rangle::\tilde{z}:\tilde{X}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}

Rule [
typ-rec-naive
] requires a name typed
\mu X.A
at the recursive definition to be typed simply
A
in the recursive body.
The associated Rule [
typ-var-naive
] then requires all names to be typed
X
, using the recursive context to make sure that the number of names concurs between recursive definition and call.
However, as the following example shows, such a combination of naive rules discards priority annotations to a point where it is possible to type processes that deadlock:

{exa}

Consider the processes

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}\mu X(x,y);\overline{x}[a]\cdot x(b);\overline{y}[c]\cdot y(d);X\langle y,x\rangle,

\displaystyle Q

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}\mu X(u,v);u(a);\overline{u}[b]\cdot v(c);\overline{v}[d]\cdot X\langle u,v\rangle.

Notice how the recursive call in
P
swaps
x
and
y
.
Let us see what happens if we unfold the recursion in
P
and
Q
:

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle\equiv\overline{x}[a]\cdot x(b);\overline{y}[c]\cdot y(d);\mu X(y,x);\overline{y}[a]\cdot y(b);\overline{x}[c]\cdot x(d);X\langle x,y\rangle

\displaystyle Q

\displaystyle\equiv u(a);\overline{u}[b]\cdot v(c);\overline{v}[d]\cdot\mu X(u,v);u(a);\overline{u}[b]\cdot v(c);\overline{v}[d]\cdot X\langle u,v\rangle

If we connect these processes on the names
x
and
u
and on
y
and
v
, we can see that the second recursive definition of this process contains a deadlock: the second receive on
y
is blocking the second send on
x
, while the second receive on
u
(waiting for the second send on
x
) is blocking the second send on
v
(for which the second receive on
y
is waiting).

Yet,
P
is typable using the naive typing rules described above:

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 189.01616pt\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 62.17097pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to132.34196pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{typ-var-naive}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$X:2\vdash X\langle y,x\rangle::x:X,y:X$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip 0.35187pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-1.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to510.37427pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\hss}\raise 2.0pt\hbox to510.37427pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}}\lower 0.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize$\cdots$}}\vskip 0.35188pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$X:2\vdash\overline{x}[a]\cdot x(b);\overline{y}[c]\cdot y(d);X\langle y,x\rangle::x:\bullet\mathbin{\otimes}^{0}\bullet\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{1}X,y:\bullet\mathbin{\otimes}^{2}\bullet\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{3}X$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to510.37427pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{typ-rec-naive}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 60.41331pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\emptyset\vdash\mu X(x,y);\overline{x}[a]\cdot x(b);\overline{y}[c]\cdot y(d);X\langle y,x\rangle::\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}x:\mu X.\bullet\mathbin{\otimes}^{0}\bullet\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{1}X,\\
y:\mu X.\bullet\mathbin{\otimes}^{2}\bullet\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{3}X\end{array}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}

Thus, these naive rules prevent the type system of
APCP
from guaranteeing deadlock freedom.

The solution is for Rule [
typ-rec
] to unfold all types.
While unfolding, the priorities in these types are lifted by a common value, denoted
t
in the rule, that must be greater than the top priority occurring in the original types (cf.
Section

3.2
).
This makes sure that any priority requirements that come up in the typing of the recursive body of the process remain valid.
The recursive context is used to record the bodies of the original folded types.
Rule [
typ-rec
] then requires that the types of names are recursive on the recursion variable used for the call.
It checks that the bodies of the types concur with the types recorded in the recursive context, up to a lift by a common value
t
(i.e., the lifter used in the application of Rule [
typ-rec
]).

{exa}

To see how the unfolding of types and the common lifter in Rule [
typ-rec
] prevents
P
from
Section

3.2
from being typable, let us attempt to find a typing derivation for
P
:

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 56.68549pt\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 301.70316pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to611.40631pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{typ-var}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$X:(\bullet\mathbin{\otimes}^{0}\bullet\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{1}X,\bullet\mathbin{\otimes}^{2}\bullet\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{3}X)\vdash X\langle y,x\rangle::\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}x:\mu X.{\uparrow^{\mkern-3.0mut}}(\bullet\mathbin{\otimes}^{0}\bullet\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{1}X),\\
y:\mu X.{\uparrow^{\mkern-3.0mut}}(\bullet\mathbin{\otimes}^{2}\bullet\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{3}X)\end{array}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip 0.35187pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-1.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to724.7773pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\hss}\raise 2.0pt\hbox to724.7773pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}}\lower 0.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize$\cdots$}}\vskip 0.35188pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$X:(\bullet\mathbin{\otimes}^{0}\bullet\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{1}X,\bullet\mathbin{\otimes}^{2}\bullet\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{3}X)\begin{array}[t]{@{}r@{}l@{}}{}\vdash&\overline{x}[a]\cdot x(b);\overline{y}[c]\cdot y(d);X\langle y,x\rangle\\
{}::&x:\bullet\mathbin{\otimes}^{0}\bullet\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{1}(\mu X.{\uparrow^{\mkern-3.0mut}}(\bullet\mathbin{\otimes}^{0}\bullet\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{1}X)),\\
&y:\bullet\mathbin{\otimes}^{2}\bullet\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{3}(\mu X.{\uparrow^{\mkern-3.0mut}}(\bullet\mathbin{\otimes}^{2}\bullet\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{3}X))\end{array}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to724.7773pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{typ-rec}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 167.61482pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\emptyset\vdash\mu X(x,y);\overline{x}[a]\cdot x(b);\overline{y}[c]\cdot y(d);X\langle y,x\rangle::\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}x:\mu X.\bullet\mathbin{\otimes}^{0}\bullet\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{1}X,\\
y:\mu X.\bullet\mathbin{\otimes}^{2}\bullet\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{3}X\end{array}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}

The application of Rule [
typ-var
] at the top here is invalid: it is impossible to find a lifter
t
that matches the priorities in the type of
x
with those in the second type assigned to
X
, while simultaneously doing the same for the type of
y
and the first type assigned to
X
.
However, if the call were
X\langle x,y\rangle
, the application of Rule [
typ-var
] would be valid.

Remark 1

(Comparison to
PCP
)
.

Consider the typing rules for sending and selection in
PCP
, in which both are blocking prefixes and do not involve continuation passing.
Note that
PCP
does not have recursion, and we thus omit the recursive context
\Omega
.

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-send-PCP}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash P::\Gamma,y:A,x:B$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\circ<\pr(\Gamma)$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to153.25781pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 23.63016pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash\overline{x}[y];P::\Gamma,x:A\mathbin{\otimes}^{\circ}B$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-sel-PCP}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash P::\Gamma,x:A_{j}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$j\in I$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\circ<\pr(\Gamma)$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to175.89226pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 19.48596pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash\overline{x}\triangleleft j;P::\Gamma,x:{\oplus}^{\circ}\{i:A_{i}\}_{i\in I}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}

These rules are similar to the Rules
[
typ-send
\ast
]
and
[
typ-sel
\ast
]
in
Figure

4
for the sugared syntax introduced in
Section

3.1
; the differences are twofold.
First, the semicolons ‘
;
’ indicate that sends and selections are indeed blocking.
Second, the rules compare the priority of the send/selection to the priorities in the context, whereas our rules compare this priority to the priorities of the continuation of the send/selection.

As anticipated, the
binding of sends and selections to continuation processes (
Section

3.1
) is consistent with typing in
APCP
.
The corresponding typing rules in
Figure

4
(bottom) are admissible using Rules [
typ-par
] and [
typ-res
].
Note that rules for the sugared receive and branching in
Section

3.1
are not necessary, because the sugared input constructs rely on
\alpha
renaming only.

Figure

4
(bottom) also includes an admissible Rule [
typ-lift
] that lifts a process’ priorities.

Theorem 2
.

Rules
[
typ-send
\ast
]
and
[
typ-sel
\ast
]
in
Figure

4
(bottom) are derivable, and Rule
[
typ-lift
]
in
Figure

4
(bottom) is admissible.

\displaystyle\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-send$\ast$}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash P::\Gamma,y:A,x:B$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\circ<\pr(A),\pr(B)$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to182.97083pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 37.09781pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash\overline{x}[y]\cdot P::\Gamma,x:A\mathbin{\otimes}^{\circ}B$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 30.27054pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\circ<\pr(A),\pr(B)$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to134.88034pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{typ-send}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash x[a,b]::x:A\mathbin{\otimes}^{\circ}B,a:\overline{A},b:\overline{B}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash P\{z/x\}::\Gamma,y:A,z:B$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to307.66629pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{typ-par}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 36.26926pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash x[a,b]\mathbin{|}P\{z/x\}::\Gamma,x:A\mathbin{\otimes}^{\circ}B,y:A,a:\overline{A},z:B,b:\overline{B}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip 0.00186pt\hbox{\hskip 35.53444pt\lower-1.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox to236.59741pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\hss}\raise 2.0pt\hbox to236.59741pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}}\lower 0.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize$2\times${{[{typ-res}]}}}}\vskip 0.00188pt\hbox{\hskip 35.53444pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash\underbrace{(\bm{\nu}ya)(\bm{\nu}zb)(x[a,b]\mathbin{|}P\{z/x\})}_{\overline{x}[y]\cdot P~\text{(cf.\ \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{n:APCP:sugar})}}::\Gamma,x:A\mathbin{\otimes}^{\circ}B$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-sel$\ast$}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash P::\Gamma,x:A_{j}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$j\in I$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\circ<\pr(A_{j})$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to181.35582pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 20.82889pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash\overline{x}\triangleleft j\cdot P::\Gamma,x:{\oplus}^{\circ}\{i:A_{i}\}_{i\in I}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 59.36662pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$j\in I$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to148.82639pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{typ-sel}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash x[b]\triangleleft j::x:{\oplus}^{\circ}\{i:A_{i}\}_{i\in I},b:\overline{A_{j}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash P\{z/x\}::\Gamma,z:A_{j}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to293.1304pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{typ-par}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 32.98451pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash x[b]\triangleleft j\mathbin{|}P\{z/x\}::\Gamma,x:{\oplus}^{\circ}\{i:A_{i}\}_{i\in I},z:A_{j},b:\overline{A_{j}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 14.17722pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to264.77597pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{typ-res}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 14.17722pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash\underbrace{(\bm{\nu}zb)(x[b]\triangleleft j\mathbin{|}P\{z/x\})}_{\overline{x}\triangleleft j\cdot P~\text{(cf.\ \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{n:APCP:sugar})}}::\Gamma,x:{\oplus}^{\circ}\{i:A_{i}\}_{i\in I}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}

Figure 5.
Proof that Rules [
typ-send
\ast
] and [
typ-sel
\ast
] are derivable (cf.
2
).

Proof 3.1

(Proof (Sketch))
.

To show the derivability of Rules
[
typ-send
\ast
]
and
[
typ-sel
\ast
]
, we give their derivations in
Figure

5
(omitting the recursive context
\Omega
).
Rule
[
typ-lift
]
is admissible:
\Omega\vdash P::\Gamma
implies
\Omega\vdash P::{\uparrow^{\mkern-3.0mut}}\Gamma
(cf. Dardha and Gay
[
DG18
]
), simply by increasing all priorities in the derivation of
P
by
t
.

2
highlights how
APCP
’s asynchrony uncovers a more primitive, lower-level view of message passing.
In the next subsection we discuss deadlock freedom, which follows from a correspondence between reduction and the transformation of typing derivations to eliminate applications of Rule [
typ-res
].
In the case of
APCP
, this requires care: binding sends and selections to continuation processes leads to applications of Rule [
typ-res
] that do not immediately correspond to reductions.

Remark 3

(Comparison To DeYoung
et al.
)
.

Our rules for sending and selection are axiomatic, whereas DeYoung
et al.
’s are in the form of Rules
[
typ-send
\ast
]
and
[
typ-sel
\ast
]
, even though their sends and selections are parallel atomic prefixes as well
[
DCPT12
]
.
While our type system is based on classical linear logic (with single-sided judgments), their type system is based on
intuitionistic
linear logic.
As a result, their typing judgments are two sided, restriction involves a single name, and there are two rules (right and left) per connective.
This way, for instance, their right rules for sending and selection are as follows (again omitting the recursive context
\Omega
for a lack of recursion):

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-send-R}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 17.7085pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\vdash P::y:A$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Delta\vdash Q::x^{\prime}:B$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to184.98172pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma,\Delta\vdash(\bm{\nu}y)(\bm{\nu}x^{\prime})(x[y,x^{\prime}]\mathbin{|}P\mathbin{|}Q)::x:A\mathbin{\otimes}B$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}\phantom{\qed}\hfill\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-sel-R}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hfill\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 31.31209pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\vdash P::x^{\prime}:A_{j}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$j\in I$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to177.79755pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\vdash(\bm{\nu}x^{\prime})(x[x^{\prime}]\triangleleft j\mathbin{|}P)::x:{\oplus}\{i:A_{i}\}_{i\in I}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}\hfill\qed

The following result is important: it shows that the type system of
APCP
is complete with respect to types, i.e., every syntactical type has a well-typed process.

Proposition 4
.

Given a type
A
, there exists
\Omega\vdash P::x:A
.

Proof 3.2

(Proof (Sketch))
.

By constructing
P
from the structure of
A
.
To this end, we define characteristc processes: a function
\chr^{x}(A)
that constructs a process that performs the behavior described by
A
on the name
x
.

\displaystyle\chr^{x}(A\mathbin{\otimes}^{\circ}B)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}\overline{x}[y]\cdot(\chr^{y}(A)\mathbin{|}\chr^{x}(B))

\displaystyle\chr^{x}(\bullet)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}\bm{0}

\displaystyle\chr^{x}(A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{\circ}B)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}x(y);(\chr^{y}(A)\mathbin{|}\chr^{x}(B))

\displaystyle\chr^{x}(\mu X.A)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}\mu X(x);\chr^{x}(A)

\displaystyle\chr^{x}({\oplus}^{\circ}\{i:A_{i}\}_{i\in I})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}\overline{x}\triangleleft j\cdot\chr^{x}(A_{j})~\text{(any $j\in I$)}

\displaystyle\chr^{x}(X)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}X\langle x\rangle

\displaystyle\chr^{x}(\&^{\circ}\{i:A_{i}\}_{i\in I})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}x\triangleright\{i:\chr^{x}(A_{i})\}_{i\in I}

For finite types, it is obvious that
\emptyset\vdash\chr^{x}(A)::x:A
.
For simplicity, we omit details about recursive types, which require unfolding.
For closed, recursive types, the thesis is obvious as well:
\emptyset\vdash\chr^{x}(\mu X.A)::x:\mu X.A
.

3.3.
Type Preservation and Deadlock Freedom

Well-typed processes satisfy protocol fidelity, communication safety, and deadlock freedom.
The former two of these properties follow from
type preservation
, which ensures that
APCP
’s semantics preserves typing.
In contrast to Caires and Pfenning
[
CP10
]
and Wadler
[
Wad12
]
, where type preservation corresponds to the elimination of (top-level) applications of Rule [
typ-cut
], in
APCP
it corresponds to the elimination of (top-level) applications of Rule [
type-res
].

APCP
’s semantics consists of reduction and structural congruence.
Since the former relies on the latter, we first need to show type preservation for structural congruence, i.e.,
subject congruence
.
The structural congruence rule that unfolds recursive definitions requires care, because the types of the unfolded process are also unfolded:

{exa}

Consider the following typed recursive definition:

\emptyset\vdash\mu X(x,y);x(a);y(b);X\langle x,y\rangle::x:\mu X.\bullet\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{0}X,y:\mu X.\bullet\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\&}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\&}$}}}^{1}X

Let us derive the typing of the unfolding of this process:
