to244.54094pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}}\lower 0.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize\ldots}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 44.04193pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}s^{\prime}}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}{!}\mathsf{Str}.{\oplus}\{\mathsf{buy}:S,\mathsf{blurb}:S^{\prime}\}},{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}m^{\prime}}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}{!}S.{?}\mathsf{B}.\mathsf{end}}\vdash_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathsf{Son}(s^{\prime},m^{\prime})}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\mathsf{B}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}

Similarly, the typings of the shop and the mother are as follows:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}s}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}{?}\mathsf{Str}.\&\{\mathsf{buy}:\overline{S},\mathsf{blurb}:\overline{S^{\prime}}\}}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathsf{Shop}(s)}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\bm{1}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}m}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}{?}S.{!}\mathsf{B}.\mathsf{end}}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathsf{Mother}(m)}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\bm{1}}

As such, the types of
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}s},{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}s^{\prime}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}m},{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}m^{\prime}}
are pairwise dual.
Hence, the typing of the entire system is simply

{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\emptyset}\vdash_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathsf{Sys}}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\mathsf{B}}.

The typing for buffered channels uses judgments of the form

{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Gamma}\vdash_{\mathtt{B}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[\vec{m}\rangle}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}S^{\prime}}\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}S}

The difference between
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}S^{\prime}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}S}
is determined by the messages in the buffer
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\vec{m}}
:
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}S}
denotes a sequence of sends and selections corresponding to the values and labels in
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\vec{m}}
, after which the type continues as
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}S^{\prime}}
.
Thus,
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}S}
denotes the type of a buffered channel’s output endpoint before it sent the messages in
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\vec{m}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}S^{\prime}}
denotes this endpoint’s current type.
This way,
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\overline{S}}
signifies the type of the buffered channel’s input endpoint, corresponding to a sequence of receives and offers corresponding to the values and labels in
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\vec{m}}
.

Figure

13
(center) gives the three typing rules for buffers.
The typing context
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Gamma}
is used in the typing of the values in the buffer.
Rule [
typ-buf
] types an empty buffer; as such,
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}S^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}S}
.
Rule [
typ-buf-send
] takes a value
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}v}
of type
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}T}
and a buffer typed
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}S^{\prime}}\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}S}
to insert
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}v}
at the start of the buffer now typed
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}S^{\prime}}\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}{!}T.S}
.
Rule [
typ-buf-sel
] takes a buffer typed
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}S^{\prime}}\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}S_{j}}
(for some
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}j}\in I
) to insert
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}j}
at the start of the buffer, now typed as
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}S^{\prime}}\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}{\oplus}\{i:S_{i}\}_{i\in I}}
.

Typing for configurations uses judgments of the form

{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Gamma}\vdash_{\mathtt{C}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\phi}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}C}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}T}

where the marker
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\phi}
indicates whether
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}C}
contains the main thread (
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\phi}={\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}}
) or only child threads (
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\phi}={\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}}
).
When composing configurations marked
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\phi_{1}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\phi_{2}}
, we compute a new marker
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\phi_{1}+\phi_{2}}
, as follows:

{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}+{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}}\mathrel{\triangleq}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}+{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}}\mathrel{\triangleq}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}+{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}}\mathrel{\triangleq}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}}\text{(${\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}+{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}}$ is undefined)}

Figure

13
(bottom) gives the typing rules for configurations.
Rule [
typ-main
] takes a term of non-session type (denoted
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\hat{T}}
) and turns it into a main thread.
Rule [
typ-child
] takes a term of type
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\bm{1}}
and turns it into a child thread.
Rule [
typ-par
] composes two configurations typed
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}T_{1}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}T_{2}}
in parallel; the rule requires one of the configurations to be typed
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\bm{1}}
and uses the other configuration’s type to type the composition
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}T_{1}+T_{2}}
:

{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}T_{1}+T_{2}}\mathrel{\triangleq}\begin{cases}{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}T_{1}}&\text{if ${\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}T_{2}}={\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\bm{1}}$}\\
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}T_{2}}&\text{if ${\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}T_{1}}={\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\bm{1}}$}\\
\text{undefined}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

As such, Rule [
typ-par
] is not defined for configurations both not typed
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\bm{1}}
; moreover, if both configurations contain a main thread, the sum of their markers is undefined, and the rule cannot be applied.
Rule [
typ-res
] types a configuration under a buffered channel with output endpoint
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}x}
and input endpoint
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}y}
.
The rule uses typing for buffers to type the buffer
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}S^{\prime}}\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}S}
.
As such, the configuration should use
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}x}
of type
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}S^{\prime}}
.
Since
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}S}
is the type of
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}x}
before it sent the messages already in the buffer,
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}y}
should be of type
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\overline{S}}
.
Note that
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}y}
may be used in the configuration, but may also appear in a message in the buffer.

4.3.
Towards a Faithful Translation of
LAST
\star
into
APCP

As already discussed, we are interested in faithfully translating
LAST
\star
into
APCP
.
We first discuss what we mean precisely by ‘faithful’.
Then, we consider some existing translations of (variants of) the
\lambda
-calculus with call-by-value semantics into (variants of) the
\pi
-calculus; we briefly discuss their status with respect to our definition of faithfulness.

Faithfulness

We shall aim for correct translations in the sense of Gorla
[
Gor10
]
, a well-established set of criteria whereby faithful translations should satisfy
operational correspondence
, a criterion that is divided into

completeness
and
soundness
properties.
Intuitively, completeness says that the target language
does enough
to represent the behavior of the source language, whereas soundness says that the target language
does not do too much
.
Alternatively, one may see completeness and soundness as properties that ensure that reduction steps are
preserved
and
reflected
by the translation, respectively.

We formulate these requirements in the specific setting of
LAST
\star
and
APCP
, by considering a translation of configurations into processes, denoted ‘
{\color[rgb]{0.6055,0,0.0117}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\cdot}\color[rgb]{0.6055,0,0.0117}\rrbracket}{z}
’, where, as usual,
z
denotes a name on which the resulting process exhibits the behavior of the source term:

Completeness:

If
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}C}\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}D}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0.6055,0,0.0117}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}C}\color[rgb]{0.6055,0,0.0117}\rrbracket}{z}\mathbin{\rightarrow^{\ast}}{\color[rgb]{0.6055,0,0.0117}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}D}\color[rgb]{0.6055,0,0.0117}\rrbracket}{z}
.

Soundness:

If
{\color[rgb]{0.6055,0,0.0117}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}C}\color[rgb]{0.6055,0,0.0117}\rrbracket}{z}\mathbin{\rightarrow^{\ast}}Q
, then there exists
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}D}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}C}\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}^{\ast}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}D}
and
Q\mathbin{\rightarrow^{\ast}}{\color[rgb]{0.6055,0,0.0117}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}D}\color[rgb]{0.6055,0,0.0117}\rrbracket}{z}
.

Soundness and completeness come in different flavors of strength; see, e.g.,
[
Pet19
]
.
Our definitions can be seen as being strictly stronger than Gorla’s, in that we do not consider a behavioral relation on target processes (typically used to abstract away from ‘junk processes’ in comparisons with
{\color[rgb]{0.6055,0,0.0117}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}D}\color[rgb]{0.6055,0,0.0117}\rrbracket}{z}
).

Existing Translations are not Sound

We are not aware of translations of
\lambda
-calculi with call-by-value semantics into the
\pi
-calculus that satisfy soundness as stated above.
To illustrate the problem, let us consider the well-known translation by Milner
[
Mil90
,
Mil92
]
.
This is a translation into a
\pi
-calculus with synchronous communication, while we seek a translation into
APCP
, which is asynchronous. This discrepancy is not crucial: the unsound reductions we will show next are not enabled by the asynchrony of
APCP
(i.e., they are also enabled in synchronous processes).

It is actually sufficient to consider Milner’s approach to translating variables and applications.
By adapting this approach to
APCP
, we obtain the translations given next.
Below, we write ‘
\_
’ to denote a fresh name of type
\bullet
; when sending names denoted ‘
\_
’, we omit binders ‘
(\bm{\nu}\_\_)
’.

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.6055,0,0.0117}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}x}\color[rgb]{0.6055,0,0.0117}\rrbracket}{z}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}(\bm{\nu}ab)(z[\_,a]\mathbin{|}b(\_,c);x[\_,c])

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.6055,0,0.0117}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M~N}\color[rgb]{0.6055,0,0.0117}\rrbracket}{z}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}(\bm{\nu}ab)(\bm{\nu}cd)\big(a(\_,e);(\bm{\nu}fg)(e[\_,f]\mathbin{|}c(\_,h);g[h,z])\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0.6055,0,0.0117}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M}\color[rgb]{0.6055,0,0.0117}\rrbracket}{b}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0.6055,0,0.0117}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}N}\color[rgb]{0.6055,0,0.0117}\rrbracket}{d}\big)

Consider the very simple term
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}x~y}
, which cannot reduce.
Yet, its translation
{\color[rgb]{0.6055,0,0.0117}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}x~y}\color[rgb]{0.6055,0,0.0117}\rrbracket}{z}
does reduce, as shown next (each step underlines the send and receive that synchronize):

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.6055,0,0.0117}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}x~y}\color[rgb]{0.6055,0,0.0117}\rrbracket}{z}

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}a_{1}b_{1})(\bm{\nu}c_{1}d_{1})\big(\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}\underline{a_{1}(\_,e_{1})};(\bm{\nu}f_{1}g_{1})(e_{1}[\_,f_{1}]\mathbin{|}c_{1}(\_,h_{1});g_{1}[h_{1},z])\\
{}\mathbin{|}(\bm{\nu}a_{2}b_{2})(\underline{b_{1}[\_,a_{2}]}\mathbin{|}b_{2}(\_,c_{2});x[\_,c_{2}])\\
{}\mathbin{|}(\bm{\nu}a_{3}b_{3})(d_{1}[\_,a_{3}]\mathbin{|}b_{3}(\_,c_{3});y[\_,c_{3}])\big)\end{array}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}(\bm{\nu}c_{1}d_{1})(\bm{\nu}a_{2}b_{2})\big(\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}(\bm{\nu}f_{1}g_{1})(\underline{a_{2}[\_,f_{1}]}\mathbin{|}c_{1}(\_,h_{1});g_{1}[h_{1},z])\\
{}\mathbin{|}\underline{b_{2}(\_,c_{2})};x[\_,c_{2}]\\
{}\mathbin{|}(\bm{\nu}a_{3}b_{3})(d_{1}[\_,a_{3}]\mathbin{|}b_{3}(\_,c_{3});y[\_,c_{3}])\big)\end{array}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}(\bm{\nu}c_{1}d_{1})(\bm{\nu}f_{1}g_{1})\big(\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}\underline{c_{1}(\_,h_{1})};g_{1}[h_{1},z]\\
{}\mathbin{|}x[\_,f_{1}]\\
{}\mathbin{|}(\bm{\nu}a_{3}b_{3})(\underline{d_{1}[\_,a_{3}]}\mathbin{|}b_{3}(\_,c_{3});y[\_,c_{3}])\big)\end{array}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}(\bm{\nu}f_{1}g_{1})(\bm{\nu}a_{3}b_{3})(g_{1}[a_{3},z]\mathbin{|}x[\_,f_{1}]\mathbin{|}b_{3}(\_,c_{3});y[\_,c_{3}])\mathrel{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.5pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\rightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.5pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\rightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.04861pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\rightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.84029pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\rightarrow}}}}

It is clear that this final term, which cannot reduce any further, cannot be reconciled with any source
LAST
\star
term.
Hence, the translation is unsound.

Besides Milner’s, other translations of
\lambda
-calculi with call-by-value semantics into (variants of) the
\pi
-calculus have been proposed by Lindley and Morris
[
LM15
]
(a precise formalization of Walder’s
[
Wad12
,
Wad14
]
), by Vasconcelos
[
Vas00
]
, and by Fowler
et al.

[
FKD+23
]
.
One of the most significant differences between them is how they translate variables: Milner uses sends; Lindley and Morris and Fowler
et al.
use forwarders; Vasconcelos uses substitutions.
None of them satisfy soundness as defined above, although Vasconcelos’ and Fowler
et al.
’s enjoy a weaker form of soundness that holds up to an appropriate behavioral equivalence.

By examining these three translations, we observe that the call-by-value semantics is overly contextual, in the sense that determining whether a subterm may reduce depends on the context.
This way, e.g., Rule [
red-lam
] (
Figure

10
) only applies to abstractions applied to values.
The semantic rules for the
\pi
-calculus are much less contextual,
so translations require additional machinery to prevent unwanted reductions.
We are not aware of translations of call-by-value
\lambda
-calculi into
\pi
that are sound, which in our view suggests that such a sound translation may not exist.

Based on this discussion, we conclude that the call-by-value semantics of
LAST
\star
does not lend itself for a faithful representation in
APCP
.
To address this issue, in the next section we will propose a variant of
LAST
with a call-by-name semantics.
This variant, denoted
\texttt{LAST}^{\mathit{n}}
, will admit a faithful (i.e., sound and complete) translation into
APCP
.

5.
\texttt{LAST}^{\mathit{n}}
and a Faithful Translation into
APCP

Here we present
\texttt{LAST}^{\mathit{n}}
, a variant of
LAST
with call-by-name semantics.
Our presentation proceeds gradually, based on the presentation we gave for
LAST
\star
(
Section

4
).
We then give a typed translation of
\texttt{LAST}^{\mathit{n}}
into
APCP
, and prove that it is faithful as discussed in
Section

4.3
.
Finally, we show how the translation can help us identify a fragment of deadlock-free
\texttt{LAST}^{\mathit{n}}
programs.

5.1.
The Language of
\texttt{LAST}^{\mathit{n}}

Terms (
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M},{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}N},\ldots
) and reduction contexts (
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R}}
):

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M},{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}N}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\kern.5ex}lr@{\kern 1.2ex}l@{\kern.5ex}lr@{}}{}::=\hfil\kern 2.15277pt&{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}x}&\text{variable}\kern 5.16663pt&\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}\hfil\kern 2.15277pt&{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{new}}}&\text{create new channel}\\
~~\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}\hfil\kern 2.15277pt&{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}()}&\text{unit value}\kern 5.16663pt&\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}\hfil\kern 2.15277pt&{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{fork}}M;N}&\text{fork ${\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M}$ in parallel to ${\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}N}$}\\
~~\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}\hfil\kern 2.15277pt&{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\lambda x.M}&\text{abstraction}\kern 5.16663pt&\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}\hfil\kern 2.15277pt&{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(M,N)}&\text{pair construction}\\
~~\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}\hfil\kern 2.15277pt&{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M~N}&\text{application}\kern 5.16663pt&\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}\hfil\kern 2.15277pt&{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}(x,y)=M\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}N}&\text{pair deconstruction}\\
~~\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}\hfil\kern 2.15277pt&{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{send}}M\,N}&\text{send ${\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M}$ along ${\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}N}$}\kern 5.16663pt&\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}\hfil\kern 2.15277pt&{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{select}}\ell\,M}&\text{select label $\ell$ along ${\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M}$}\\
~~\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}\hfil\kern 2.15277pt&{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{recv}}M}&\text{receive along ${\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M}$}\kern 5.16663pt&\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}\hfil\kern 2.15277pt&{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{case}}M\mathinner{\mathtt{of}}\{i:M\}_{i\in I}}&\text{offer labels in $I$ along ${\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M}$}\\
~~\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}\hfil\kern 2.15277pt&{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}M;N}&\text{close ${\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M}$}\kern 5.16663pt&\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}\hfil\kern 2.15277pt&{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M{\lBrace N/x\rBrace}}&\text{explicit substitution}\end{array}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R}}}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\kern.5ex}l@{}}{}::=\hfil\kern 2.15277pt&{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[\cdot]}}\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R}}~M}\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{send}}M\,{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R}}}\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{recv}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R}}}\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}(x,y)={\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R}}\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}M}\\
~~\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}\hfil\kern 2.15277pt&{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{select}}\ell\,{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R}}}\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{case}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R}}\mathinner{\mathtt{of}}\{i:M\}_{i\in I}}\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R}};M}\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R}}{\lBrace M/x\rBrace}}\end{array}

Figure 14.
Term syntax and reduction contexts for
\texttt{LAST}^{\mathit{n}}
.

To define
\texttt{LAST}^{\mathit{n}}
, we start from
LAST
\star
and modify its semantics.
We only need to change the definitions of reduction contexts and reduction in
Figures

10
and
11
.
For the reader’s convenience,
Figure

14
gives the syntax of
\texttt{LAST}^{\mathit{n}}
terms and reduction contexts;
Section

A.1
contains a full, self-contained definition of the language and type system of
\texttt{LAST}^{\mathit{n}}
.

The crux of the required changes is that call-by-name semantics applies abstractions more eagerly, and not only when arguments are values.
To be precise, term reduction changes Rules [
red-lam
] and [
red-pair
] as follows:

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{red-lam}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 45.39828pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to98.79657pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\lambda x.M)~\underline{N}}\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M\{\underline{N}/x\}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{red-pair}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 81.14752pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to170.29506pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}(x,y)=(\underline{N_{1}},\underline{N_{2}})\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}M}\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M\{\underline{N_{1}}/x,\underline{N_{2}}/y\}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}

Above, we underline modified parts: instead of requiring values, the rules allow arbitrary terms.
Moreover, to enforce the eager application of these rules, we remove from the definition of reduction contexts the clauses
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}v~{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}({\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R}},M)}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(v,{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R}})}
.
That is,
\texttt{LAST}^{\mathit{n}}
disallows terms in parameter positions to reduce.

{exa}

We consider the term from
Section

4.1
, now using the call-by-name semantics of
\texttt{LAST}^{\mathit{n}}
:

{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\big(\lambda x.x~(\lambda y.y)\big)~\big((\lambda w.w)~(\lambda z.z)\big)}\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\big((\lambda w.w)~(\lambda z.z)\big)~(\lambda y.y)}\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\lambda z.z)~(\lambda y.y)}\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\lambda y.y}

Notice how here the function on
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}x}
is applied
before
its parameter is evaluated as in
Section

4.1
.

Following the same call-by-name spirit, message passing in
\texttt{LAST}^{\mathit{n}}
transmits unevaluated terms, instead of only values as in
LAST
\star
.
To accommodate this, we replace the two reduction-context clauses for
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{send}}}
with the clause
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{send}}M\,{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R}}}
, and modify Rule [
red-send
] accordingly:

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{red-send}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 100.93942pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to209.87885pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\nu{x[\vec{m}\rangle y}({\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{F}}\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{send}}M\,x\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}]\parallel C)}\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\nu{x[\vec{m},M\rangle y}({\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{F}}\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}x\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}]\parallel C)}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}

As is to be expected, there are subtle differences between the semantics of
\texttt{LAST}^{\mathit{n}}
and
APCP
.
In order to deal with these discrepancies, while enabling the desired completeness and soundness results, we enrich
\texttt{LAST}^{\mathit{n}}
with
explicit substitutions
and
closed sessions
.

Explicit Substitutions

Term reductions [
red-lam
] and [
red-pair
] substitute variables for terms, regardless of the position of these variables.
To mimic this in
APCP
, we would have to be able to substitute the translations of variables for translations of terms.
Although this is possible when those variables occur in reduction contexts, there is no mechanism for representing this in
APCP
when they occur in different contexts (where their translations are blocked by prefixes).

One way of dealing with this issue is to equate translations up to substitutions, using the so-called
substitution lifting

[
TCP12
]
.
Here, we opt for an alternative, more direct treatment based on
explicit substitutions
(see, e.g.,
[
LM99
]
).
Intuitively, explicit substitutions delay variable substitution until those variables occur in reduction contexts.
To incorporate explicit substitutions, we proceed as follows:

•

We add explicit substitutions to the syntax of terms
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M{\lBrace N/x\rBrace}}
and configurations
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}C{\lBrace N/x\rBrace}}
.
These are not meant to be used when writing programs, instead appearing and disappearing as programs reduce (runtime syntax).
That is, they appear when abstractions are applied and pairs deconstructed:

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{red-lam}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 45.21764pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to98.43527pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\lambda x.M)~N}\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M{\lBrace N/x\rBrace}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{red-pair}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 95.0363pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to198.07262pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}(x,y)=(N_{1},N_{2})\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}M}\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M{\lBrace N_{1}/x,N_{2}/y\rBrace}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}

Explicit substitutions disappear when they meet the substituted variable, as per the following rule:

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{red-subst}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 23.73323pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to55.46646pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}x{\lBrace N/x\rBrace}}\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}N}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}

•

We add reduction contexts
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R}}{\lBrace N/x\rBrace}}
and configuration contexts
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{G}}{\lBrace N/x\rBrace}}
.
We then define a structural congruence for terms, denoted
\equiv_{\mathtt{M}}
, with a single rule that enables extruding the scope of explicit substitutions across reduction contexts:

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{sc-sub-ext}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 37.25865pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}x}\notin\fv({\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R}})$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to122.04974pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}({\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R}}\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}]){\lBrace N/x\rBrace}}\equiv_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R}}\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M{\lBrace N/x\rBrace}\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}]$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}

We close term reduction under this structural congruence:

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{red-lift-sc}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M}\equiv_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M^{\prime}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M^{\prime}}\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}N^{\prime}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}N^{\prime}}\equiv_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}N}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to156.96013pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 59.93427pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M}\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}N}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}

We also add rules to the structural congruence for configurations that lift explicit substitutions in terms and enable scope extrusion on the level of configurations, respectively:

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{sc-conf-sub}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 55.62206pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to119.24411pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\phi\,(M{\lBrace N/x\rBrace})}\equiv_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\phi\,M){\lBrace N/x\rBrace}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{sc-conf-sub-ext}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 34.57254pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}x}\notin\fv({\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{G}})$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to117.16364pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}({\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{G}}\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}C\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}]){\lBrace N/x\rBrace}}\equiv_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{G}}\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}C{\lBrace N/x\rBrace}\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}]$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}

•

When moving terms between threads, we need to make sure that we do not affect variables that are bound by explicit substitutions.
As such we define a specific form of thread context, denoted
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{\hat{F}}}
, that disallows the hole to appear under explicit substitutions.
Configuration reductions [
red-fork
], [
red-send
], and [
red-recv
] (cf.
Figure

11
) then use these specific contexts.

•

To type explicit substitutions, we add the following typing rules for terms and configurations:

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-sub}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}x}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}T}\vdash_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}U}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Delta}\vdash_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}N}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}T}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to124.73566pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 22.07486pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Delta}\vdash_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M{\lBrace N/x\rBrace}}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}U}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-conf-sub}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}x}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}T}\vdash_{\mathtt{C}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\phi}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}C}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}U}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Delta}\vdash_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}N}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}T}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to122.93987pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 22.07484pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Delta}\vdash_{\mathtt{C}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\phi}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}C{\lBrace N/x\rBrace}}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}U}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}

{exa}

We revisit
Section

5.1
, this time using explicit substitutions:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\big(\lambda x.x~(\lambda y.y)\big)~\big((\lambda w.w)~(\lambda z.z)\big)}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\big(x~(\lambda y.y)\big){\lBrace\big((\lambda w.w)~(\lambda z.z)\big)/x\rBrace}}

\displaystyle\equiv_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(x{\lBrace\big((\lambda w.w)~(\lambda z.z)\big)/x\rBrace})~(\lambda y.y)}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\big((\lambda w.w)~(\lambda z.z)\big)~(\lambda y.y)}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(w{\lBrace(\lambda z.z)/w\rBrace})~(\lambda y.y)}\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\lambda z.z)~(\lambda y.y)}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}z{\lBrace(\lambda y.y)/z\rBrace}}\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\lambda y.y}

{exa}

[The Bookshop Scenario in
\texttt{LAST}^{\mathit{n}}
]

Recall the bookshop scenario introduced in
Section

2.2
, already illustrated by
Section

4.1
for
LAST
\star
.
Although the end result is the same, the CbN semantics and explicit substitutions of
\texttt{LAST}^{\mathit{n}}
do change the behavior of the system (
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\mathsf{Sys}}
) compared to its behavior under
LAST
\star
.

We start to illustrate this behavior by reconsidering how the system starts by setting up channels; as we will see, not all explicit substitutions can be immediately resolved (we sometimes abbreviate unchanged threads using “
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\ldots}
”):

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\mathsf{Sys}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}(s,s^{\prime})=\mathinner{\mathtt{new}}\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}\ldots}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\bm{\nu}y[\epsilon\rangle y^{\prime}){\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}(s,s^{\prime})=(y,y^{\prime})\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}\ldots}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\bm{\nu}y[\epsilon\rangle y^{\prime}){\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{fork}}\mathsf{Shop}(s);\ldots{\lBrace y/s,y^{\prime}/s^{\prime}\rBrace}}

\displaystyle\equiv_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\bm{\nu}y[\epsilon\rangle y^{\prime})\big(({\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{fork}}\mathsf{Shop}(s);\ldots){\lBrace y/s,y^{\prime}/s^{\prime}\rBrace}\big)}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\bm{\nu}y[\epsilon\rangle y^{\prime})\big(({\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}(m,m^{\prime})=\mathinner{\mathtt{new}}\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}\ldots\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathsf{Shop}(s)){\lBrace y/s,y^{\prime}/s^{\prime}\rBrace}\big)}

\displaystyle\equiv_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\bm{\nu}y[\epsilon\rangle y^{\prime})\big(({\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\ldots\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}(\textit{title},s_{1})=\mathinner{\mathtt{recv}}(s{\lBrace y/s\rBrace})\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}\ldots){\lBrace y^{\prime}/s^{\prime}\rBrace}\big)}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\bm{\nu}y[\epsilon\rangle y^{\prime})\big(({\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\ldots\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}(\textit{title},s_{1})=\mathinner{\mathtt{recv}}y\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}\ldots){\lBrace y^{\prime}/s^{\prime}\rBrace}\big)}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}^{2}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\bm{\nu}y[\epsilon\rangle y^{\prime})\big((\bm{\nu}z[\epsilon\rangle z^{\prime})({\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{fork}}\mathsf{Mother}(m);\mathsf{Son}(s^{\prime},m^{\prime})){\lBrace z/m,z^{\prime}/m^{\prime},y^{\prime}/s^{\prime}\rBrace}\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathsf{Shop}(y)\big)}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\bm{\nu}y[\epsilon\rangle y^{\prime})\big((\bm{\nu}z[\epsilon\rangle z^{\prime})\big({\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\mathsf{Son}(s^{\prime},m^{\prime}){\lBrace z^{\prime}/m^{\prime},y^{\prime}/s^{\prime}\rBrace}\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathsf{Mother}(m){\lBrace z/m\rBrace}\big)\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathsf{Shop}(y)\big)}

\displaystyle\equiv_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\bm{\nu}y[\epsilon\rangle y^{\prime})\big((\bm{\nu}z[\epsilon\rangle z^{\prime})\big({\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\ldots\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}(x,m_{1})=\mathinner{\mathtt{recv}}(m{\lBrace z/m\rBrace})\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}\ldots\big)\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathsf{Shop}(y)\big)}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\bm{\nu}y[\epsilon\rangle y^{\prime})\big((\bm{\nu}z[\epsilon\rangle z^{\prime})\big({\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\ldots\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}(x,m_{1})=\mathinner{\mathtt{recv}}z\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}\ldots\big)\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathsf{Shop}(y)\big)}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\bm{\nu}y[\epsilon\rangle y^{\prime})\big((\bm{\nu}z[\epsilon\rangle z^{\prime})\big({\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\mathsf{Son}(s^{\prime},m^{\prime}){\lBrace z^{\prime}/m^{\prime},y^{\prime}/s^{\prime}\rBrace}\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathsf{Mother}(z)\big)\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathsf{Shop}(y)\big)}\mathrel{\triangleq}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathsf{Sys}^{1}}

At this point, the explicit substitutions on the main thread cannot be resolved, as neither of
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}m^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}s^{\prime}}
appears under a reduction context in
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathsf{Son}_{s^{\prime},m^{\prime}}}
.
We continue the example by examining the behavior of the son at this point (abbreviating some substitutions using “
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\ldots}
”):

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathsf{Son}(s^{\prime},m^{\prime}){\lBrace z^{\prime}/m^{\prime},y^{\prime}/s^{\prime}\rBrace}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}s^{\prime}_{1}=\mathinner{\mathtt{send}}\text{``Dune''}\,s^{\prime}\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}\ldots{\lBrace z^{\prime}/m^{\prime},y^{\prime}/s^{\prime}\rBrace}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}s^{\prime}_{2}=\mathinner{\mathtt{select}}\mathsf{buy}\,s^{\prime}_{1}\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}\ldots{\lBrace\mathinner{\mathtt{send}}\text{``Dune''}\,s^{\prime}/s^{\prime}_{1},z^{\prime}/m^{\prime},y^{\prime}/s^{\prime}\rBrace}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}m^{\prime}_{1}=\mathinner{\mathtt{send}}s^{\prime}_{2}\,(m^{\prime}{\lBrace z^{\prime}/m^{\prime}\rBrace})\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}\ldots{\lBrace\mathinner{\mathtt{select}}\mathsf{buy}\,s^{\prime}_{1}/s^{\prime}_{2},\mathinner{\mathtt{send}}\text{``Dune''}\,s^{\prime}/s^{\prime}_{1},y^{\prime}/s^{\prime}\rBrace}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}m^{\prime}_{1}=\mathinner{\mathtt{send}}s^{\prime}_{2}\,z^{\prime}\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}\ldots{\lBrace\ldots\rBrace}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}(\textit{book},m^{\prime}_{2})=\mathinner{\mathtt{recv}}(m^{\prime}_{1}{\lBrace\mathinner{\mathtt{send}}s^{\prime}_{2}\,z^{\prime}/m^{\prime}_{1}\rBrace})\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}\ldots{\lBrace\ldots\rBrace}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}(\textit{book},m^{\prime}_{2})=\mathinner{\mathtt{recv}}(\mathinner{\mathtt{send}}s^{\prime}_{2}\,z^{\prime})\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}\ldots{\lBrace\ldots\rBrace}}\mathrel{\triangleq}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathsf{Son}^{1}(s^{\prime},z^{\prime})}

This chain of explicit substitutions cannot be resolved further:
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}s^{\prime}_{2}}
does not appear under a reduction context.
In fact, they will not be resolved until the son has delegated his (pending) access to
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}z^{\prime}}
to his mother, along with his pending messages in the form of explicit substitutions, as shown by the sequence leading to process
\mathsf{Sys}^{2}
in
Figure

15
.

\displaystyle\hphantom{{}\mathbin{\rightarrow}_\mathtt{C}{}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathsf{Sys}^{1}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}^{5}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\bm{\nu}y[\epsilon\rangle y^{\prime})\big((\bm{\nu}z[\epsilon\rangle z^{\prime})\big({\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\mathsf{Son}^{1}(s^{\prime},z^{\prime})\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathsf{Mother}(z)\big)\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathsf{Shop}(y)\big)}

\displaystyle\equiv_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\bm{\nu}y[\epsilon\rangle y^{\prime})\big((\bm{\nu}z^{\prime}[\epsilon\rangle z)\big({\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}(\textit{book},m^{\prime}_{2})=\mathinner{\mathtt{recv}}(\mathinner{\mathtt{send}}s^{\prime}_{2}\,z^{\prime})\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}\ldots\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\ldots\big){\lBrace\ldots\rBrace}\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\ldots\big)}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\bm{\nu}y[\epsilon\rangle y^{\prime})\big(\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}(\bm{\nu}z^{\prime}[s^{\prime}_{2}\rangle z)\big({\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}(\textit{book},m^{\prime}_{2})=\mathinner{\mathtt{recv}}z^{\prime}\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}\ldots\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}(x,m_{1})=\mathinner{\mathtt{recv}}z\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}\ldots\big)\\
{\lBrace\ldots\rBrace}\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\ldots\big)\end{array}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\bm{\nu}y[\epsilon\rangle y^{\prime})\big((\bm{\nu}z^{\prime}[\epsilon\rangle z)\big({\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\ldots\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}(x,m_{1})=(s^{\prime}_{2},z)\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}\ldots{\lBrace\ldots\rBrace}\big)\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\ldots\big)}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\bm{\nu}y[\epsilon\rangle y^{\prime})\big((\bm{\nu}z^{\prime}[\epsilon\rangle z)\big(\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\ldots\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}x_{1}=\mathinner{\mathtt{send}}\text{visa}\,(x{\lBrace s^{\prime}_{2}/x,\ldots\rBrace})\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}\ldots{\lBrace z/m_{1}\rBrace}\big)\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\ldots\big)\end{array}\end{array}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\bm{\nu}y[\epsilon\rangle y^{\prime})\big((\bm{\nu}z^{\prime}[\epsilon\rangle z)\big({\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\ldots\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}x_{1}=\ldots(s^{\prime}_{2}{\lBrace\mathinner{\mathtt{select}}\mathsf{buy}\,s^{\prime}_{1}/s^{\prime}_{2},\ldots\rBrace})\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}\ldots\big)\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\ldots\big)}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\bm{\nu}y[\epsilon\rangle y^{\prime})\big((\bm{\nu}z^{\prime}[\epsilon\rangle z)\big({\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\ldots\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}x_{1}=\ldots\,(\mathinner{\mathtt{select}}\mathsf{buy}\,(s^{\prime}_{1}{\lBrace\mathinner{\mathtt{send}}\text{``Dune''}\,s^{\prime}/s^{\prime}_{1},y^{\prime}/s^{\prime}\rBrace}))\\
\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}\ldots\big)\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\ldots\big)\end{array}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\bm{\nu}y[\epsilon\rangle y^{\prime})\big((\bm{\nu}z^{\prime}[\epsilon\rangle z)\big({\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\ldots\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}x_{1}=\ldots(\ldots(\mathinner{\mathtt{send}}\text{``Dune''}\,(s^{\prime}{\lBrace y^{\prime}/s^{\prime}\rBrace})))\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}\ldots\big)\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\ldots\big)}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\bm{\nu}y[\epsilon\rangle y^{\prime})\big((\bm{\nu}z^{\prime}[\epsilon\rangle z)\big({\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\ldots\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}x_{1}=\ldots(\ldots(\ldots y^{\prime}))\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}\ldots\big)\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\ldots\big)\mathrel{\mathrel{\triangleq}}\mathsf{Sys}^{2}}

Figure 15.

The Bookshop Scenario in
\texttt{LAST}^{\mathit{n}}
(cf.
Section

5.1
).
We abbreviate unchanged terms and substitutions using “
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\ldots}
”.

At this point, the system behaves roughly as in
Section

4.1
: there is no further session delegation (as we just witnessed between the son and the mother), so upcoming explicit substitutions can be resolved straightforwardly.

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathsf{Sys}^{2}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}^{3}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\bm{\nu}y^{\prime}[\text{visa},\mathsf{buy},\text{``Dune''}\rangle y)\big((\bm{\nu}z^{\prime}[\epsilon\rangle z)\big(\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}(\textit{book},m^{\prime}_{2})=\mathinner{\mathtt{recv}}z^{\prime}\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}\ldots\\
\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{let}}x_{1}=y^{\prime}\mathinner{\mathtt{in}}\ldots{\lBrace z/m_{1}\rBrace}\big)\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathsf{Shop}(y)\big)\end{array}}

From here, the thread representing the mother can finally perform her son’s two original outputs, followed by her own (omitted for brevity).

Closed Sessions

Both in
\texttt{LAST}^{\mathit{n}}
and
APCP
, closed sessions have no associated behavior.
This entails some management on both sides to garbage collect unused variables with type
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\mathsf{end}}
.
As a result, translating terms with variables requires two forms: one for regular variables, and another for variables typed
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\mathsf{end}}
.
Because such a translation can get burdensome, we opt to add a form of explicit closing to
\texttt{LAST}^{\mathit{n}}
.
Once both endpoints of a channel have been closed, the channel and its buffer can be garbage collected.
This treatment relieves us from translating unused variables, in such a way that the translation of terms with variables comes in one intuitive form only.
To formalize this idea, we proceed as follows:

•

We add terms for endpoint closing
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}M;N}
and a reduction context
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R}};N}
.
Similar to
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{fork}}}
, the term
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}N}
is an explicit continuation for when the endpoint is closed.
We define behavior on the level of configurations by adding a new reduction rule:

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{red-close}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 108.68251pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to225.36504pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\bm{\nu}x[\vec{m}\rangle y)({\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{F}}\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}x;M\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}]\parallel C)}\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\bm{\nu}\square[\vec{m}\rangle y)({\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{F}}\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}]\parallel C)}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}

Here, ‘
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\square}
’ is a special runtime endpoint variable: it indicates that the endpoint
x
has been closed and so the variable is not used anywhere inside the enclosed configuration.
Then, buffered channels can be garbage collected when both endpoints have been closed, as enabled by the following reduction:

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{red-res-nil}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 31.94945pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to71.8989pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\bm{\nu}\square[\epsilon\rangle\square)C}\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}C}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}

•

We type the session closing construct by replacing Rule [
typ-end
] with the following:

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-close}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Gamma}\vdash_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\mathsf{end}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Delta}\vdash_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}N}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}T}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to113.09192pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 8.41173pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Delta}\vdash_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}M;N}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}T}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}

•

We add a new session type for endpoints that have been already closed.
With a slight abuse of notation, we denote it as
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\square}
.
We disallow using this type in the typing of terms, leaving it only to appear in the typing of buffered channels.
To ensure that closed endpoint variables are not used in configurations, we add side conditions to Rule [
typ-res
]:
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}x}={\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\square}
if and only if
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}S^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\square}
and similarly for
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}y}
.
The following two new rules enable typing partially closed buffered channels:

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-buf-end-L}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 27.61703pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to63.23409pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\emptyset}\vdash_{\mathtt{B}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[\epsilon\rangle}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\mathsf{end}}\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\square}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\mbox{\scriptsize{{{[{typ-buf-end-R}]}}}}\\
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 27.61703pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to63.23409pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\emptyset}\vdash_{\mathtt{B}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[\epsilon\rangle}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\square}\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\mathsf{end}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}

{exa}

We illustrate the explicit closing of sessions in
\texttt{LAST}^{\mathit{n}}
in the context of the bookstore scenario introduced in
Section

2.2
.
Consider the system where all session interactions have taken place, and all three threads are ready to close their sessions:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathsf{Sys}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}^{\ast}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\bm{\nu}y[\epsilon\rangle y^{\prime})\big((\bm{\nu}z[\epsilon\rangle z^{\prime})\big({\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}z^{\prime};\text{book}(\text{``Dune''})\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}z;\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}y^{\prime}\big)\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}y\big)}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\triangleq}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathsf{Sys}^{c}}

Once again, notation
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\text{book}(\textit{title})}
is syntactic sugar for a lookup function.
The order in which endpoints are closed is unimportant; here, we execute the
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}}
primitives from left to right.

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathsf{Sys}^{c}}

\displaystyle\equiv_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(\bm{\nu}y[\epsilon\rangle y^{\prime})\big((\bm{\nu}z^{\prime}[\epsilon\rangle z)\big({\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}z^{\prime};\text{book}(\text{``Dune''})\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}z;\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}y^{\prime}\big)\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}y\big)}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\text{book}(\text{``Dune''})\parallel(\bm{\nu}y[\epsilon\rangle y^{\prime})\big((\bm{\nu}\square[\epsilon\rangle z){\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}z;\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}y^{\prime}\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}y\big)}

\displaystyle\equiv_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\text{book}(\text{``Dune''})\parallel(\bm{\nu}y[\epsilon\rangle y^{\prime})\big((\bm{\nu}z[\epsilon\rangle\square){\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}z;\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}y^{\prime}\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}y\big)}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\text{book}(\text{``Dune''})\parallel(\bm{\nu}y[\epsilon\rangle y^{\prime})\big((\bm{\nu}\square[\epsilon\rangle\square){\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}y^{\prime}\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}y\big)}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\text{book}(\text{``Dune''})\parallel(\bm{\nu}y[\epsilon\rangle y^{\prime})\big({\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}y^{\prime}\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}y\big)}

\displaystyle\equiv_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\text{book}(\text{``Dune''})\parallel(\bm{\nu}y^{\prime}[\epsilon\rangle y)\big({\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}y^{\prime}\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}y\big)}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\text{book}(\text{``Dune''})\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\,()\parallel(\bm{\nu}\square[\epsilon\rangle y){\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}y}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\text{book}(\text{``Dune''})\parallel(\bm{\nu}\square[\epsilon\rangle y){\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}y}

\displaystyle\equiv_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\text{book}(\text{``Dune''})\parallel(\bm{\nu}y[\epsilon\rangle\square){\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}\mathinner{\mathtt{close}}y}

(
\ast
)

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\text{book}(\text{``Dune''})\parallel(\bm{\nu}\square[\epsilon\rangle\square){\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}()}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\text{book}(\text{``Dune''})\parallel{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\lozenge}()}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}{\mathchoice{\scriptstyle}{\scriptstyle}{\scriptscriptstyle}{}\blacklozenge}\,\text{book}(\text{``Dune''})}

\ast
).

(book) a primitive non-linear type that can be weakened/contracted at will and is self dual.

Type Preservation

\texttt{LAST}^{\mathit{n}}
satisfies the expected correctness properties for session-typed languages: protocol fidelity and communication safety.
Both properties follow directly from
type preservation
.

Theorem 5.0

(Type Preservation for
\texttt{LAST}^{\mathit{n}}
)
.

Given
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Gamma}\vdash_{\mathtt{C}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\phi}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}C}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}T}
, if
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}C}\equiv_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}D}
or
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}C}\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}D}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Gamma}\vdash_{\mathtt{C}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\phi}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}D}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}T}
.

Proof 5.1
.

By proving subject congruence (
\equiv_{\mathtt{C}}
preserves typing) and subject reduction (
\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{C}}
preserves typing) separately.
In both cases, we first prove separately subject congruence and subject reduction on the term level (
\equiv_{\mathtt{M}}
and
\mathbin{\rightarrow}_{\mathtt{M}}
, respectively).
These results follow by induction on the derivation of the structural congruence or reduction.
Below we give a few interesting cases;
Section

A.2
contains detailed proofs.

•

For
\equiv_{\mathtt{M}}
, the only interesting case is the only base case, i.e.,
Rule
[
sc-sub-ext
]
:

{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}x}\notin\fv({\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R}})\implies{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}({\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R}}\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}]){\lBrace N/x\rBrace}}\equiv_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R}}\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M{\lBrace N/x\rBrace}\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}]

We apply induction on the structure of the reduction context
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R}}
.
As an interesting, representative case, consider
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R}}={\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}L{\lBrace{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R^{\prime}}}/y\rBrace}}
.
Assuming
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}x}\notin\fv({\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R}})
, we have
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}x}\notin\fv({\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}L})\cup\fv({\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R^{\prime}}})
.
We apply inversion of typing:

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}y}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}U}\vdash_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}L}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}T}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Delta},{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}x}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}U^{\prime}}\vdash_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R^{\prime}}}\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}]:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}U}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to163.14235pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{typ-sub}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 19.8435pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Delta},{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}x}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}U^{\prime}}\vdash_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}L{\lBrace({\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R^{\prime}}}\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}])/y\rBrace}}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}T}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Delta^{\prime}}\vdash_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}N}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}U^{\prime}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 19.8435pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to243.55548pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{typ-sub}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 68.31793pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Delta},{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Delta^{\prime}}\vdash_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}(L{\lBrace({\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R^{\prime}}}\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}])/y\rBrace}){\lBrace N/x\rBrace}}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}T}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}

We can derive:

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Delta},{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}x}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}U^{\prime}}\vdash_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R^{\prime}}}\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}]:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}U}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Delta^{\prime}}\vdash_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}N}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}U^{\prime}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to155.10638pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{typ-sub}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 23.06575pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Delta},{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Delta^{\prime}}\vdash_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}({\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R^{\prime}}}\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}]){\lBrace N/x\rBrace}}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}U}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}

Since
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}x}\notin\fv({\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R^{\prime}}})
, by Rule
[
sc-sub-ext
]
,
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}({\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R^{\prime}}}\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}]){\lBrace N/x\rBrace}}\equiv_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R^{\prime}}}\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M{\lBrace N/x\rBrace}\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}]
.
Then, by the IH,
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Delta},{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Delta^{\prime}}\vdash_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R^{\prime}}}\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M{\lBrace N/x\rBrace}\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}]:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}U}
.
Hence, we can conclude the following:

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}y}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}U}\vdash_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}L}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}T}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Delta},{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Delta^{\prime}}\vdash_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R^{\prime}}}\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M{\lBrace N/x\rBrace}\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}]:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}U}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to178.51587pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{typ-sub}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 19.84352pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Delta},{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Delta^{\prime}}\vdash_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}L{\lBrace({\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{R^{\prime}}}\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M{\lBrace N/x\rBrace}\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}])/y\rBrace}}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}T}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}

It is straightforward to see that this reasoning works in opposite direction as well.

Rule

red-send

:

This case follows by induction on the structure of
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{\hat{F}}}

The inductive cases follow from the IH straightforwardly.
The fact that the hole in
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\mathpzc{\hat{F}}}
does not occur under an explicit substitution guarantees that we can move
{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M}

Note that the derivation of
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Gamma}\vdash_{\mathtt{B}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[\vec{m}\rangle}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}{!}T.\mathsf{end}}\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}S}

{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\vec{m}}
(IH
2
), we derive
{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Delta_{1}}\vdash_{\mathtt{B}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[M,\vec{m}\rangle}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\mathsf{end}}\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}S}
:

–

{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}\vec{m}}
is empty, it follows by inversion of typing that

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Delta_{1}}\vdash_{\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}M}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}T}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 31.11703pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to70.23407pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{typ-buf}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\emptyset}\vdash_{\mathtt{B}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[\epsilon\rangle}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\mathsf{end}}\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\mathsf{end}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to136.59549pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{typ-buf-send}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 19.78833pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\Delta_{1}}\vdash_{\mathtt{B}}{\color[rgb]{0.0977,0,0.543}[M\rangle}:{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}\mathsf{end}}\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.7422,0.2891,0}{!}T.\mathsf{end}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}

By IH

–
